# Risk of Spread

*Latest analysis data cutoff: 10 October 2026. Page refreshed: 21 October 2026.*

This page is in beta and still a work in progress. Please direct feedback to [Collaboratory@who.int](mailto:Collaboratory@who.int). This page is subject to the [disclaimer](#risk-spread-page-bottom) below.

:::{note} WHO Rapid Risk Assessment
For the latest risk assessment, see the WHO [Rapid Risk Assessment: Ebola disease caused by Bundibugyo virus (v4)](https://www.who.int/publications/m/item/who-rapid-risk-assessment-ebola-disease-caused-by-bundibugyo-virus--democratic-republic-of-the-congo-v4), 20 August 2026.

WHO's overall risk and confidence ratings by geography:

| Geography | Overall risk | Confidence |
|---|---|---|
| Democratic Republic of the Congo | **Very High** | Moderate |
| Countries sharing land borders with the Democratic Republic of the Congo | **High** | Moderate |
| Rest of the African Region | **Low** | Moderate |
| Global | **Low** | High |

## Synthesizing evidence

### State of the evidence

As of 19 October 2026, WHO has reported 9,637 confirmed cases in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) across 66 health zones in eight provinces.[^who-afro-2026-1] Sixteen analyses are included, the most recent with a snapshot date of 10 October 2026.[^epistorm-2026b-front-matter-1] Eleven provide risk-of-spread estimates: nine address cross-border or international importation, and two address spread within DRC.[^okingo-2026-abstract-1][^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-abstract-1] Some share authors and/or data sources, so they are not fully independent. For example, Klein et al. and Verheyden & Mudogo (2026b) both use Flowminder data.[^klein-2026-2-methods-4][^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-24]

### How the estimates compare

Analyses that rank countries by their likelihood of importing cases from the DRC outbreak largely agree on the highest-risk countries and diverge further down the list. Chamla et al. place Uganda (94.2%) and South Sudan (69.3%) far above DRC's other neighboring countries,[^chamla-2026-summary-4] Epistorm's October update keeps that order, with Uganda carrying 98.0% of relative risk outside DRC and South Sudan 53.5% of the remainder once Uganda is set aside,[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-4][^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-12] and Fanelli et al. (2026a) rank South Sudan at highest importation risk.[^fanelli-2026a-discussion-13] Rwanda and Burundi are low in Chamla et al., but Fanelli et al. (2026a) rate them high and attribute the difference to spatial heterogeneity and conflict-adjusted travel times,[^fanelli-2026a-discussion-12] and Li et al. rate them very high, even above South Sudan.[^li-2026-results-4]

The main divergence is methodological. Air-traffic analyses elevate international hubs such as Belgium, France, South Africa, Kenya, and the UAE,[^kinoshita-2026-abstract-2] or France, Italy, and the UK within Europe,[^fanelli-2026b-abstract-3] while analyses combining land and air mobility place a largely contiguous belt of African countries at highest risk.[^fanelli-2026a-results-1] Assessments focused on North Africa and Europe rate introduction as very low to low,[^bouhentala-2026-abstract-2] and the US assessment rates overall risk to the US population as low, based on an extremely low likelihood of transmission.[^richard-2026-abstract-2] Rankings hold across scenarios and assumptions even where absolute estimates do not.[^okingo-2026-body-9][^fanelli-2026a-results-4] From June to October the top tier holds while the list of higher-risk African countries lengthens, with Kenya, the Central African Republic, Tanzania, and Ethiopia following South Sudan in Epistorm's October update,[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-12] but differing methods and time horizons make it unclear whether this reflects outbreak spread or re-estimation.

The importations into Kenya and Nigeria fall within the modeled higher-risk tiers.[^fanelli-2026a-results-1] As of 19 October, Kenya has confirmed nine cases, eight of them among relatives and hospital staff of the imported index case who were already in quarantine.[^who-afro-2026-2] It ranks behind South Sudan in Epistorm's October update and appears as a hub in air-based analyses.[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-12][^kinoshita-2026-abstract-2] The Nigeria case, a traveler from Uganda, was confirmed 13 days after symptom onset,[^who-afro-2026-3] and Nigeria is one of three geographically distant countries in the Fanelli et al. (2026a) high-risk quartile.[^fanelli-2026a-results-1]

Within DRC, Okingo et al. separate importation from amplification risk. Health zones near the affected area rank high for introduction, while high potential for sustained transmission appears mainly in more distant urban centers, including Kinshasa.[^okingo-2026-body-1][^okingo-2026-body-12]

### Key uncertainties and assumptions

Four assumptions recur across the analyses, and where scenarios were tested, rankings were more robust than absolute estimates.[^okingo-2026-body-9][^fanelli-2026a-results-4]

First, mobility inputs are a recurring source of uncertainty in the importation estimates. Land-border data are scarce,[^fanelli-2026a-discussion-7] and the Uganda district model uses a single 10-day window that may miss movements through unofficial crossings.[^walekhwa-2026-4-discussion-3] Fanelli et al. (2026a) assume infected people travel only during the latent phase, although some with early or mild symptoms may still travel.[^fanelli-2026a-discussion-2] Chamla et al. identify their travel-while-infectious parameters as the principal source of uncertainty,[^chamla-2026-methods-15] and varying them moves South Sudan by about 20 percentage points but Uganda by under 5, because Uganda's probability is already near the upper end of its range.[^chamla-2026-results-8]

Second, source-area incidence is uncertain. Reported counts mix transmission with expanded testing and reporting backlogs, so the rise in DRC counts cannot be read directly as acceleration.[^bouhentala-2026-3-results-11] Epistorm estimates that about 69% of symptomatic cases have been reported,[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-12] and Bouhentala & Kadir note that reporting dates in public aggregate data do not represent symptom onset.[^bouhentala-2026-4-discussion-3] Confirmed DRC cases have also risen roughly sixfold, from 1,561 on 4 July[^fanelli-2026a-introduction-1] to 9,637 on 19 October,[^who-afro-2026-1] so estimates fitted to earlier counts, such as the 4 July cutoff in Fanelli et al. (2026a), predate most of this growth.

Third, importation estimates do not by themselves describe onward spread. Arrival probability measures importation, not onward spread.[^kinoshita-2026-airline-flow-based-network-and-effective-distance-for-22] Containment depends on surveillance, diagnostic capacity, workforce, and infection prevention and control.[^gashema-2026-13] Fanelli et al. (2026a) note that operational preparedness is not determined solely by structural readiness indicators.[^fanelli-2026a-discussion-11]

Fourth, some analyses do not model border or screening measures. Epistorm does not simulate entry screening or restrictions on travelers[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-10] and Kinoshita et al. do not account for enhanced screening,[^kinoshita-2026-airline-flow-based-network-and-effective-distance-for-21] so absolute risks in these analyses are probably overstated, with conditional rankings less affected.[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-11] Chamla et al. instead include point-of-entry detection sensitivity.[^chamla-2026-methods-2]

Some analyses also rest on structural choices of their own. Kinoshita et al. borrow a hazard parameter from the 2013-2016 West African epidemic,[^kinoshita-2026-airline-flow-based-network-and-effective-distance-for-23] and Li et al. treat all cumulative cases as potentially infectious, a worst-case assumption.[^li-2026-methods-10]

### What would reduce uncertainty

First, outbreak-specific, repeated mobility data, including movements through unofficial crossings, would help most, since Chamla et al., Walekhwa et al., and Fanelli et al. (2026a) all name mobility gaps.[^chamla-2026-discussion-13][^walekhwa-2026-4-discussion-3][^fanelli-2026a-discussion-7] [Flowminder](https://www.flowminder.org/resources/publications-reports/drc-reports-publications), whose movement data are already used in several DRC analyses,[^okingo-2026-figure-1-introduction-risk-and-sustained-transmission-2][^klein-2026-2-methods-4][^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-24] is one source of this kind of mobility data.

Second, case counts aggregated by date of report cannot be used to separate transmission from changing ascertainment,[^bouhentala-2026-abstract-5] and Bouhentala & Kadir could not estimate onset-to-isolation delays, serial intervals, or health-zone-specific growth from them.[^bouhentala-2026-4-discussion-12] Chamla et al. recommend that future iterations integrate line lists and contact-tracing data.[^chamla-2026-discussion-13]

Third, measures of operational readiness, rather than structural indicators alone, for top-ranked destinations would show how well each could contain an imported case, which importation rankings cannot.[^gashema-2026-13] Useful measures include laboratory capacity, infection prevention and control, and contact tracing,[^fanelli-2026a-discussion-18] isolation capacity and sample transport,[^klein-2026-5-conclusion-2] and referral readiness,[^bouhentala-2026-4-discussion-4] since the size of an outbreak seeded by an importation depends on containment capacity as well as on the importation itself.[^chamla-2026-discussion-10][^fanelli-2026a-discussion-11]

### Open questions

Several analyses call for iterative updating as data accrue,[^chamla-2026-discussion-6][^kinoshita-2026-conclusion-1] including weekly model reruns based on the latest mobility and case data.[^walekhwa-2026-4-discussion-14] Whether travel by people with early or mild symptoms,[^fanelli-2026a-discussion-2] humanitarian, military, or charter flights,[^fanelli-2026a-discussion-6] or changing land mobility[^walekhwa-2026-4-discussion-3] would materially shift rankings remains unresolved. Walekhwa et al. also call for outbreak-specific estimates of BDBV biological parameters, particularly the infectious period and serial interval.[^walekhwa-2026-4-discussion-15]

### What this means for the response

based on current information, Uganda and South Sudan are among the countries most likely to experience case importations,[^chamla-2026-summary-4][^fanelli-2026a-discussion-13] but importation risk and readiness to contain a case do not necessarily go together. South Sudan pairs high importation risk with documented gaps across all five WHO preparedness domains,[^chamla-2026-discussion-10] whereas Uganda's repeated importations have not led to sustained community transmission.[^bouhentala-2026-4-discussion-9] In Kenya, eight of nine confirmed cases were already in quarantine when they developed symptoms.[^who-afro-2026-2] Zambia, Ethiopia, and Tanzania combine high exposure with stronger capacity, while Burundi and the Central African Republic share South Sudan's pairing of high importation risk and readiness gaps.[^fanelli-2026a-discussion-13] Fanelli et al. (2026a) likewise frame prioritizing surveillance and response as depending both on where imported infections are most likely to occur and on whether recipient countries are prepared to detect and contain them.[^fanelli-2026a-abstract-3]

For DRC, the evidence supports attention beyond the eastern hotspots, including Kinshasa and other urban health zones where amplification potential is high despite distance from the outbreak.[^okingo-2026-body-12] Klein et al. likewise find that allocation of diagnostic capacity cannot be based only on current confirmed cases, because the major value comes from stopping future lineages,[^klein-2026-4-discussion-23] and Verheyden & Mudogo (2026b) caution that low observed local incidence should not be treated as zero risk where mobility and reporting gaps remain.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-conclusion-2] Spread into South Sudan, or amplification in Kampala, Kigali, or Kinshasa, would reshape exposure patterns, including importation pressure to Europe.[^fanelli-2026b-abstract-2][^fanelli-2026b-text-5]

For more distant African destinations, the Nigeria case, in which the patient first sought care at a private facility four days after symptom onset and was confirmed nine days later, illustrates why Bouhentala & Kadir identify early recognition in routine clinical care as the priority for preparedness.[^who-afro-2026-3][^bouhentala-2026-4-discussion-10]

For destinations outside Africa, risk of introduction is assessed as low overall,[^bouhentala-2026-abstract-2][^richard-2026-abstract-2] although France and Belgium rank high as arrival points in Kinoshita et al. and Li et al.[^kinoshita-2026-abstract-2][^li-2026-results-4] For North Africa and Europe, Bouhentala & Kadir recommend focused preparedness, including fully documented exposure histories, rapid isolation, validated diagnostics, protected clinical care, and contact management, rather than reliance on border screening,[^bouhentala-2026-abstract-4] whereas Li et al. say their high-risk countries, including France and Belgium, warrant enhanced entry screening.[^li-2026-results-4]

[^fanelli-2026a-discussion-13]: [Fanelli et al. South Sudan, the country at highest importation risk, combined intermediate-low emergency preparedness and response with limited healthcare system capacity, raising concerns that imported cases could more readily generate secondary transmission."

[^fanelli-2026a-discussion-12]: [Fanelli et al. [^kinoshita-2026-airline-flow-based-network-and-effective-distance-for-23]: [Kinoshita, Suzuki, & Yoneoka (2026) "Airline-flow-based network and effective distance for hazard estimation"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.06.01.26354569): "Because no airline-mediated importation had been reported so far as of 28 May 2026, we used a hazard parameter estimated using the 2013-2016 West African EVD epidemic. Here, we assume that the relationship between effective distance and EVD importation observed in 2013-2016 was transferable to the current BVD outbreak."

[^li-2026-methods-10]: [Li et al. [^chamla-2026-discussion-13]: [Chamla et al. (2026) "Discussion"](https://www.thelancet.com/journals/laninf/article/PIIS1473-3099(26)00320-8/fulltext): "Future iterations should integrate line lists, contact-tracing data and dynamic mobility estimates."

[^bouhentala-2026-abstract-5]: [Bouhentala & Kadir (2026) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.07.19.26358411): "Public reporting-date series cannot separate transmission from changing ascertainment, but they showed no sustained decline."

[^bouhentala-2026-4-discussion-12]: [Bouhentala & Kadir (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.07.19.26358411): "Reporting dates do not represent symptom onset, and the analysis could not estimate time-varying reproduction numbers, serial intervals, onset-to-isolation delays, household secondary-attack rates, health-zone-specific growth, or intervention effects."

[^chamla-2026-discussion-10]: [Chamla et al. (2026) "Discussion"](https://www.thelancet.com/journals/laninf/article/PIIS1473-3099(26)00320-8/fulltext): "Although South Sudan's importation probability is lower than Uganda's, documented deficits across all five WHO preparedness domains mean any importation would generate a substantially larger secondary outbreak. Closing these gaps within 4 to 8 weeks is the principal operational window."

[^fanelli-2026a-discussion-18]: [Fanelli et al. (2026a) "DISCUSSION"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.07.21.26358330): "In the absence of licensed vaccines and specific therapeutics against Bundibugyo Ebola virus, strengthening surveillance, laboratory capacity, infection prevention and control, contact tracing, risk communication, and cross-border coordination remains the cornerstone of preparedness."

[^klein-2026-5-conclusion-2]: [Klein et al. (2026) "5. Conclusion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The scale of diagnostic roll-out has to be matched by the human system that makes a result actionable: sample transport, laboratory staff, result return, isolation capacity, contact tracing, and safe-burial teams."

[^bouhentala-2026-4-discussion-4]: [Bouhentala & Kadir (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.07.19.26358411): "Passenger volumes, traveller occupations, deployment rosters, medevac activity, and country-specific laboratory and referral readiness were incomplete. Country ratings were therefore comparative judgements rather than calibrated probabilities."

[^chamla-2026-discussion-6]: [Chamla et al. (2026) "Discussion"](https://www.thelancet.com/journals/laninf/article/PIIS1473-3099(26)00320-8/fulltext): "These projections require iterative recalibration as new data emerge."

[^kinoshita-2026-conclusion-1]: [Kinoshita, Suzuki, & Yoneoka (2026) "Conclusion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.06.01.26354569): "Our analysis provides an early, and reproducible assessment of potential long-distance dissemination pathways. These projections can support timely international situational awareness and should be updated as additional epidemiological information becomes available, including reports of exported cases."

[^walekhwa-2026-4-discussion-14]: [Walekhwa et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.06.22.26356210): "Real-time updating, running the model weekly with new DTM data and case counts, would transform this retrospective analysis into a prospective decision support tool, as advocated by Kraemer et al. (2018) for real-time epidemic response."

[^fanelli-2026a-discussion-6]: [Fanelli et al. (2026a) "DISCUSSION"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.07.21.26358330): "Third, air-mediated importation risk only considered commercial passenger flights and did not account for humanitarian, military, or charter flights."

[^walekhwa-2026-4-discussion-15]: [Walekhwa et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.06.22.26356210): "The practical implication is that early outbreak response should include rapid estimation of the infectious period and serial interval for the specific Ebola strain. Investing in field studies to characterise these biological parameters, through contact tracing data and serial interval analysis, could yield greater returns than refining operational protocols that are already performing adequately, a strategy advocated by Chowell et al. (2019) for Ebola preparedness."

[^klein-2026-4-discussion-23]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Second, allocation cannot be based only on current confirmed cases. Because the major value comes from stopping future lineages, the target is a probability distribution over future importations."

[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-conclusion-2]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Conclusion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Higher-risk health zones warrant stronger school-linked surveillance and minimum readiness; low observed local incidence should not be treated as zero risk when mobility and reporting gaps remain; and school closure should be considered against realistic educational, protection and community-exposure alternatives."

[^fanelli-2026b-abstract-2]: [Fanelli et al. (2026b) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.05.31.26354511): "Relative exposure patterns remained largely unchanged under localized transmission in eastern DRC and border-spillover scenarios. Expansion into South Sudan generated a first structural increase in importation pressure to Europe through the connectivity associated with Juba, while hypothetical amplification in Kampala, Kigali, and Kinshasa substantially increased importation pressure and reshaped exposure patterns across Europe."

[^bouhentala-2026-4-discussion-9]: [Bouhentala & Kadir (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.07.19.26358411): "This pattern shows that cross-border importation and healthcare-associated secondary transmission can occur without progression to sustained community transmission when surveillance, isolation, contact management, and specialised care function effectively."

[^fanelli-2026a-abstract-3]: [Fanelli et al. (2026a) "ABSTRACT"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.07.21.26358330): "Anticipating where imported infections are most likely to occur, and whether recipient countries are prepared to detect and contain them, is essential for prioritizing surveillance and response."

[^fanelli-2026b-text-5]: [Fanelli et al. (2026b) "Text"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.05.31.26354511): "Kampala and Kigali amplification increased exposure in Northern Europe, with Kampala producing a &gt;120-fold increase in importation pressure relative to localized transmission in DRC. Kinshasa amplification instead primarily reinforced exposure in Western Europe (~80-fold increase)."

[^bouhentala-2026-4-discussion-10]: [Bouhentala & Kadir (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.07.19.26358411): "The most important preparedness failure would be delayed recognition in routine clinical care, not failure of thermal or border screening to detect an asymptomatic incubating traveller."

[^bouhentala-2026-abstract-4]: [Bouhentala & Kadir (2026) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.07.19.26358411): "Preparedness in North Africa and Europe should prioritise complete exposure histories, rapid isolation, validated diagnostics, protected clinical care, and contact management rather than reliance on border screening."

[^who-afro-2026-1]: [World Health Organization Regional Office for Africa (2026) "Ebola Bundibugyo Virus Disease Outbreak, Democratic Republic of the Congo | Uganda: Weekly External Situation Report 23, Data as of 19 October 2026"](https://www.afro.who.int/countries/nigeria/publication/ebola-bundibugyo-virus-disease-outbreak-democratic-republic-congo-uganda-weekly-external-situation): "As of 19 October 2026, the BVD outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo has affected 66 health zones across eight provinces, following the recent expansion into Wapinda Health Zone in Nord-Ubangi Province. Since External Situation Report #22, an additional 572 confirmed cases and 265 confirmed deaths have been reported, bringing the cumulative total to 9637 confirmed cases, including 4646 confirmed deaths [crude case fatality ratio (CFR) 48.2%]."

[^who-afro-2026-2]: [World Health Organization Regional Office for Africa (2026) "Ebola Bundibugyo Virus Disease Outbreak, Democratic Republic of the Congo | Uganda: Weekly External Situation Report 23, Data as of 19 October 2026"](https://www.afro.who.int/countries/nigeria/publication/ebola-bundibugyo-virus-disease-outbreak-democratic-republic-congo-uganda-weekly-external-situation): "Since the previous report, Kenya has confirmed eight additional cases of BVD, bringing the cumulative total to nine cases, including one death (CFR: 11.1%). All eight new cases were epidemiologically linked to the imported index case and were already under quarantine when they developed symptoms. The secondary cases included four family members and four hospital staff."

[^who-afro-2026-3]: [World Health Organization Regional Office for Africa (2026) "Ebola Bundibugyo Virus Disease Outbreak, Democratic Republic of the Congo | Uganda: Weekly External Situation Report 23, Data as of 19 October 2026"](https://www.afro.who.int/countries/nigeria/publication/ebola-bundibugyo-virus-disease-outbreak-democratic-republic-congo-uganda-weekly-external-situation): "On 20 October 2026, the Federal Ministry of Health of Nigeria announced a confirmed case of BVD in the Federal Capital Territory, Abuja. The case involved a woman who reportedly travel from Uganda to Nigeria on 3 October 2026. She developed symptoms on 7 October 2026 and sought care at a private health facility on 11 October 2026. Following clinical deterioration, laboratory testing confirmed BVD on 20 October 2026."


## Individual analyses

:::::{div}
:class: exclude-from-outline evidence-synthesis-tabs
::::{tab-set}
:::{tab-item} Epistorm (10 Oct 2026)
### Epistorm (10 Oct 2026)<br>Ebola Bundibugyo Virus — DRC · Spread Risk Assessment #9 · Local Transmission Scenarios

[Read the analysis](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html)

### Overview

This analysis estimates transmission parameters, the current transmission level and the true burden of the outbreak in DRC, and projects its course to 30 November 2026.[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-1] It re-runs a metapopulation transmission model (GLEAM, with a care pathway from community to hospital to treatment centre) fitted to the rise in reported cases between 31 July and 30 September 2026.[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-2][^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-3] It compares three scenarios in which 50%, 60% or 75% of symptomatic cases are isolated.[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-4] The 60% scenario is best supported, with the effective reproduction number close to 1 (0.95 in October).[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-5][^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-6] That scenario projects 10,465 reported cases by 31 October.[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-7] International spread is out of scope.[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-8]

### Estimate

**Headline (60% isolation scenario, DRC nationally):** The early-phase R₀ is 1.87 [1.69 – 2.09] and the doubling time is 13.2 [9.8 – 17.6] days.[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-11] About 69% [53 – 88] of all symptomatic cases since the start of the epidemic have been reported.[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-12] Rt was 0.98 in August and 0.95 in October.[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-6] The model gives 9,069 [8,531 – 9,727] reported cases on 10 October against 8,978 observed.[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-13] By 31 October it projects 15,225 [12,151 – 19,694] true symptomatic cases, of which 10,465 [9,433 – 11,835] are expected to be reported, with about 83 [47 – 150] new symptomatic cases per day.[^epistorm-2026b-projections-1] By 30 November it projects 12,259 [10,446 – 15,059] reported cases, corresponding to about 17,741 true symptomatic cases.[^epistorm-2026b-projections-2]

**Range across isolation scenarios:** R₀ rises from 1.71 [1.57 – 1.87] at 50% to 2.13 [1.88 – 2.36] at 75% isolation.[^epistorm-2026b-two-stage-calibration-scenario-selection-and-posteriors-5] October Rt is 1.00 under 50%, 0.95 under 60% and 0.86 under 75%.[^epistorm-2026b-effective-reproduction-number-1-june-30-1] On 31 October, reported cumulative cases are 10,979 [9,786 – 12,219] (50%) and 9,859 [8,948 – 11,084] (75%), and true cumulative cases are 16,493 [12,781 – 24,114] (50%) and 12,941 [10,507 – 15,280] (75%).[^epistorm-2026b-projections-3][^epistorm-2026b-projections-4] On 30 November, reported cumulative cases are 13,382 [11,132 – 15,950] (50%) and 11,071 [9,528 – 13,442] (75%), and true cumulative cases are 19,728 [14,711 – 29,049] (50%) and 14,399 [11,174 – 18,147] (75%).[^epistorm-2026b-projections-5][^epistorm-2026b-projections-6] The scenarios differ more in true burden than in reported counts.[^epistorm-2026b-projections-7] Reported cases per day stay roughly flat under 50% isolation (about 80 on 31 October and 80 on 30 November), decline slowly under 60% (64 and 56) and decline faster under 75% (47 and 34).[^epistorm-2026b-projections-8]

**Probabilities by 30 November:** Reported cases are very likely to exceed 10,000 under 50% and 60% isolation (> 99% and 99%) and likely under 75% (82%), and exceeding 20,000 reported cases is very unlikely in all scenarios (< 1%).[^epistorm-2026b-projections-9] The probability of 50 or more new cases per day is 94% under 50%, 79% under 60% and 36% under 75% isolation, and transmission is not expected to end by 30 November under any scenario.[^epistorm-2026b-projections-10]

### Methods

**GLEAM care-pathway transmission model:** GLEAM is an Africa-wide metapopulation network coupled by daily commuting matrices and origin-destination passenger flows from IATA and OAG.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-4] Within each basin, a Legrand-like SEIHFR scheme represents community, health-facility-only and isolation pathways.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-5] Transmission is overdispersed, with a small share of cases generating most secondary infections (dispersion k = 0.1).[^epistorm-2026b-methods-6] A gradual isolation ramp of 1 percentage point per day starts after each area's intervention trigger.[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-10] It produces reported and true case trajectories for DRC nationally, under 50%, 60% and 75% isolation.

**Two-stage Approximate Bayesian Computation (ABC) calibration:** Stage 1 selects trajectories consistent with early cross-border onsets in Uganda and early activity in Beni. Stage 2 fits the reported DRC counts.[^epistorm-2026b-two-stage-calibration-scenario-selection-and-posteriors-2] The fit targets the increase in reported cases since 31 July, because earlier cases were partly added retrospectively.[^epistorm-2026b-two-stage-calibration-scenario-selection-and-posteriors-3] Isolation scenarios are compared with Bayes factors.[^epistorm-2026b-two-stage-calibration-scenario-selection-and-posteriors-4] The scenario ranking is the same for ABC tolerances between 3% and 10%.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-7]

**Effective reproduction number (Rt) estimation:** Rt is the Cori estimator applied to simulated true daily onsets, with a 7-day sliding window and a gamma serial interval (mean 15.3 days, SD 9.3 days).[^epistorm-2026b-methods-8] It is computed for DRC nationally.

### Assumptions

**Isolation level and ascertainment:** Each scenario sets a target isolation level (50%, 60% or 75%).[^epistorm-2026b-methods-18] Reporting is modelled as proportional to isolation, α(t) = c × Q(t), with every isolated case assumed reported and ascertainment capped at 1.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-19] Projections assume each scenario's isolation level is maintained and that ascertainment keeps tracking isolation.[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-16] Because the epidemic level before 31 July is only indirectly constrained, the overall ascertainment and true case numbers carry wide intervals.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-20]

**Isolation ramp timing:** Each DRC basin starts a ramp of 1 percentage point per day when its cumulative onsets first reach 80. Bunia has a fixed start on 20 May 2026, and Uganda is held at 80% throughout.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-21] Isolation and case-finding timings are applied homogeneously within each basin, although real-world coverage varies by location and over time.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-22]

**Reporting delay:** Onset-to-report follows a gamma distribution with mean 5 days and SD 3.5 days, held constant over time.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-23] SDs of 2.5 and 5 days give the same results.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-24] Changes in the delay over time and batch updates of reported counts are not modelled.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-25]

**Transmissibility and natural history:** Overdispersion k = 0.1 is assumed.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-26] The baseline R₀ split is Community : HCF : Funeral at 0.44 : 0.22 : 0.34.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-27] Residual nosocomial transmission in the isolation pathway is 10% of the routine HCF rate, an assumed value.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-28] The latent period is U(5, 10) days.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-29] The onset-to-admission time is 2 days on the isolation track.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-30]

**Seed and priors:** The epidemic originates in Ituri with 5 infectious cases on the inferred start date.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-31] The R₀ prior is U(1.2, 4.0) and the start-date prior is U(1 February, 15 March 2026).[^epistorm-2026b-methods-32]

**Mobility and interventions:** Latent individuals are fully mobile, while 50% of symptomatic community cases remain mobile and isolated and hospitalised cases do not travel.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-33] Mobility is held static, with no behavioural reduction, border closure or seasonality in transmissibility.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-34] No vaccination is modelled.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-35]

**Calibration tolerance:** Samples with distance ≤ 5% are accepted.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-36] The scenario ranking is the same for tolerances between 3% and 10%.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-7]

### Limitations

**Reporting-process simplifications:** Reporting of new cases is assumed proportional to isolation with a constant factor per trajectory.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-37] The onset-to-report delay is an assumption held constant, and changes in the delay over time and batch updates of reported counts are not modelled.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-25]

**Weak constraint on early epidemic size:** The level of the epidemic before 31 July is only indirectly constrained, so overall ascertainment and the true number of cases carry wide intervals.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-20] The early epidemic size is only loosely constrained.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-38] Cases detected before and around the May alert were partly added retrospectively, so the cumulative level up to the first August report is not used as a fitting target.[^epistorm-2026b-two-stage-calibration-scenario-selection-and-posteriors-3]

**Hospital and isolation occupancy not compared:** Model occupancy of the hospital and isolation compartments is not compared with the reported number of patients in isolation in this report, pending checks of the definition of the reported figure and treatment-centre length of stay.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-39]

**Homogeneity and static mobility:** Isolation and case-finding timings are applied homogeneously within each basin; real-world coverage varies.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-22] Mobility is held static, with no behavioural reduction, no border closure and no seasonality in transmissibility.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-34]

**Validation and precision:** Projections are validated on a single held-out count (10 October).[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-17] Fewer simulations are accepted under 75% isolation, so its estimates are less precise.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-40]

**Trend interpretation:** The data capture the slowdown itself, not yet its outcome.[^epistorm-2026b-projections-11]

### Data sources

**Primary outbreak data:** DRC cumulative reported cases from 31 July to 30 September 2026 were used in the fit, and the 10 October count was held out.[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-14] The fit uses increments since 31 July at seven situation-report dates (+882, +1,701, +2,039, +2,576, +3,268, +4,099 and +4,626 cases).[^epistorm-2026b-methods-9] The reported-count projection is anchored at 3,674 cases.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-10] The 10 October count is 8,978.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-11] The same situation reports give 4,336 cumulative deaths across 65 health zones, and 945 patients in isolation.[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-9] Stage 1 calibration uses 2–4 early cross-border onsets in Uganda and 2–80 early cumulative onsets in Beni.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-12] Uganda's 20 laboratory-confirmed cases (2 deaths) and one case in Kenya are mentioned as context.[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-15]

**Auxiliary data:** Mobility inputs are IATA Passenger Intelligence Services and OAG origin-destination passenger flows plus daily commuting matrices.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-4] Dwell times and severity values come from the literature, including Wamala et al. for the 2007–2008 Uganda BVD outbreak.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-13][^epistorm-2026b-methods-14] Isolation-pathway case-finding timings follow the 2018 North Kivu RVEAP response.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-15] The model structure follows the Legrand-type SEIHFR scheme.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-16] The 5-day report delay is an assumption, described as a typical delay from onset to hospital or ETC admission.[^epistorm-2026b-methods-17]
:::

:::{tab-item} Epistorm (7 Oct 2026)
### Epistorm (7 Oct 2026)<br>Ebola Bundibugyo Virus — DRC · Spread Risk Assessment · International Dissemination

[Read the analysis](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html)

### Overview

The analysis does not estimate outbreak size. It describes where an exported case is most likely to be detected, given that at least one export occurs, rather than asserting that an export will occur.[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-1][^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-2] It combines air-travel and short-scale commuting flows into a mobility-only relative importation risk over a two-week horizon.[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-3] Uganda alone carries 98.0 % of the non-DRC relative risk.[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-4] With Uganda removed, South Sudan leads the remaining international destinations with ~53 %, followed by Kenya (~12 %) and the Central African Republic (~7 %).[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-5] The absolute probability of any single international export remains small.[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-6]

### Estimate

The analysis does not report an outbreak size estimate; it reports conditional relative importation risk. Across neighbouring countries, 99.0 % of the city-level relative risk sits in five cities: Kasese (UGA, 58.4 %), Pakuba (UGA, 28.6 %), Arua (UGA, 10.8 %), Juba (SSD, 0.9 %), and Rumbek (SSD, 0.15 %).[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-11] In the international tail, South Sudan takes 53.5 % (Juba 45.9 %, Rumbek 7.6 %), followed by Kenya (12.0 %), the Central African Republic (6.9 %), the UAE (3.2 %), Tanzania (2.9 %), and Ethiopia (1.7 %).[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-12] Africa retains 86.3 % of the tail, followed by Europe 5.2 %, the Middle East 4.8 %, Asia 2.6 %, and the Americas 0.9 %.[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-13] Beyond the UAE, intercontinental destinations sit at roughly 1 % or less.[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-14] These values are shares of export probability if an export occurs, not absolute probabilities of export.[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-15] No uncertainty intervals or scenario ranges are reported. The ranking applies to the two-week window from the 7 October 2026 snapshot.

### Methods

**Mobility-only relative importation risk (GLEAM-based):** The approach follows the GLEAM (Global Epidemic and Mobility) computational framework.[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-3] For each destination, the model estimates the probability that an infected person from the affected area travels there, given at least one exported case, and outputs a relative risk distribution normalised to sum to one.[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-4] Inputs are IATA and OAG origin–destination air passenger flows plus short-scale commuting patterns between adjacent subpopulations.[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-5] No transmission model is used.[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-1] The framework is unchanged from earlier reports in the series (Reports #1 to #4 and #6).[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-3] The same underlying distribution is presented in two views: city-level risk across DRC's neighbouring countries before renormalisation, and an international tail with only Uganda removed and the remainder renormalised over 120 destinations reachable by air.[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-6]

### Assumptions

**Conditional framing and horizon:** Values are conditional on at least one international export occurring, and describe how export probability is distributed across destinations, not the absolute probability for any destination.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-1] They describe where an exported case would likely land within the next two weeks.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-2] For the tail, Uganda is removed and the remainder renormalised to sum to one.[^epistorm-2026a-neighbouring-country-cities-2]

**Incubation period:** The incubation period of 2–21 days (average ~11 days) constrains the window within which an exported case could travel while infectious or pre-symptomatic.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-3]

**Traveller homogeneity:** Sociodemographic attributes of travellers are not incorporated, and travel probability within a catchment is treated as independent of these attributes and of specific location within the catchment.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-4] Estimates apply to general-population traffic and exclude repatriation flights, responder evacuations, and other ad-hoc operational movements.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-5]

**Independence of importations:** Importations are modelled as statistically independent events, and a multi-case cluster travelling together counts as a single importation event.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-6] The authors state the model may then under-count distinct exposure events while correctly counting distinct geographic seedings.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-7]

**No border-control measures:** The model does not simulate entry screening, restrictions on travellers from the affected area, or air-service suspensions.[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-10] Such measures would in particular reduce the absolute probability of export, while the conditional ranking is less sensitive but still affected.[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-11]

**No transmission dynamics:** Risk estimates reflect the probability of a single importation and do not account for sustained local transmission in the receiving location.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-8]

### Limitations

**Mobility-only baseline:** The report uses the mobility framework only; no transmission-dynamics model is applied, and the values should not be read as forecasts of future case counts at any destination.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-9] The model does not account for the probability of sustained local transmission in the receiving location.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-8] Absolute likelihood of any international export, downstream local transmission, and outbreak burden at source are out of scope.[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-16]

**Border-control measures not modelled:** The analysis does not model country-level border-control measures, which would in particular affect absolute risk.[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-17] Conditional destination ranking is less sensitive but still affected, so the values should be interpreted as the mobility-only baseline.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-10]

**Traveller attributes and movement types:** The model does not incorporate sociodemographic attributes of travellers, which may modulate both exposure risk at origin and care-seeking behaviour at destination.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-11] Importation risk from repatriation flights, responder evacuations, or other ad-hoc operational movements is not part of the analysis.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-12]

**Independent importations and clusters:** Related-case clusters are counted as one importation event, so the model may under-count distinct exposure events.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-13]

**Informal cross-border movement:** Mobility data may not fully capture informal cross-border movement, which is substantial within the immediate cross-border region but is not the focus of the international-tail analysis.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-14]

**Small absolute risk not quantified:** The unconditional probability of international export remains small and is further modulated by border-control interventions not captured in the model.[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-15]

### Data sources

**Primary outbreak data:** The report uses DRC data as of 7 October 2026[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-7], but the dataset type and sample size are not described. The report also cites a CFR of ≈ 48% in DRC as of 10 October 2026 (8,978 cases, 4,336 deaths).[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-8]

**Auxiliary data:** Global air travel patterns come from the International Air Transport Association (IATA) and the Official Airline Guide (OAG), giving passenger-level origin–destination flows between international airports.[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-7] These are complemented by short-scale commuting patterns capturing daily movements between adjacent subpopulations.[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-8] The incubation period used is 2–21 days, average ~11 days.[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-9] The report also notes that BDBV was first isolated in Uganda in 2007.[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-8]
:::

:::{tab-item} Verheyden and Mudogo (12 Aug 2026)
### Verheyden and Mudogo (12 Aug 2026)<br>Incidence-weighted force of infection for predicting first reported health-zone cases during the 2026 Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak: a rolling-origin evaluation

[Read the analysis](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text)

### Overview

This analysis estimates which currently unaffected health zones in four DRC provinces are at highest near-term risk of reporting their first confirmed Bundibugyo virus case, in order to support prepositioning of response capacity. Four connectivity specifications — no connectivity term, road-distance, a gravity score, and an incidence-weighted force-of-infection (FOI) term — were fitted within an identical Bayesian hierarchical hazard architecture across 106 health zones in four affected provinces.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-abstract-1] The FOI specification achieved the highest top-10 hit rate (42.6%), though on a global discrimination metric (AUC-PR), a model with no connectivity term performed as well as or better than any connectivity specification (0.437 vs. 0.409 for FOI).[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-abstract-2]

### Estimate

This analysis does not estimate total outbreak size or case counts; its output is a ranked list of at-risk health zones by their estimated daily hazard of first reporting a confirmed case. The top-ranked zone, Rethy (Ituri), had a posterior median daily hazard of 0.095 (95% CrI [0.018, 0.295]), corresponding to roughly a 75% chance of reporting a first case within 14 days if current conditions persisted; the 20th-ranked zone's daily hazard corresponds to roughly 4% over the same window.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-1] On the pre-specified primary metric (top-10 hit rate), FOI was highest (42.6%), followed by OSRM-distance and gravity (both 32.8%), then baseline (29.5%).[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-2] On AUC-PR (a secondary metric), the baseline model with no connectivity term performed as well as or better than every connectivity specification (baseline 0.437 vs. FOI 0.409, gravity 0.399, OSRM-distance 0.239), and FOI's advantage over baseline on this metric was not distinguishable from zero (−0.027, 95% CI [−0.274, 0.200]).[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-3] Across the kernel sensitivity grid of 8 alternative combinations, the resulting zone ranking correlated with the primary specification's ranking at Spearman ρ = 0.90–0.99, with top-10 overlap of 9 or 10 out of 10 zones in every combination tested.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-4]

### Methods

**Discrete-time hazard model with incidence-weighted force-of-infection (FOI):** A discrete-time hazard model represents spatial connectivity through the observed incidence in every currently affected zone, propagated to at-risk zones through a road-network distance-decay kernel, referred to as an incidence-weighted spatial force-of-infection (FOI) hazard model.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-1-introduction-1] Daily case data come from the INRB-UMIE BDBV2026-Data repository; static covariates include WorldPop population estimates, OpenStreetMap/HOT health-facility points, Complex Crisis Vulnerability Index, and province-level ACLED conflict events; road-network travel times come from a national OSRM graph.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-5] CDR/mobility data were not used, as DRC is not among the countries for which this is available.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-6] The model is applied to the 106 health zones in Ituri, Nord-Kivu, Haut-Uele, and Tshopo provinces. A sensitivity analysis varied the FOI kernel's decay scale (φ ∈ {30, 60, 120} minutes) and smoothing window (1, 3, 7 days) across a grid of 8 alternative combinations, reporting ranking stability rather than re-selecting a best-performing specification.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-7]

**Architecture-matched comparison of four connectivity specifications:** Four specifications of the connectivity covariate were compared within the identical model architecture: (1) a baseline with no connectivity term (static covariates only), (2) road-distance (log travel time to the nearest already-affected zone), (3) a population-weighted gravity score summed over all affected zones, and (4) the incidence-weighted FOI term as the primary specification.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-8] Fitting all four within the identical architecture isolates what each connectivity term contributes beyond the static covariates and beyond each other.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-9]

**Rolling-origin pseudo-prospective evaluation:** Eight weekly origins from 21 May to 9 July 2026 were used, each refit on data available through that origin and scored against zones reporting a first case within the following 14 days; top-10 hit rate — the proportion of true next-invading zones ranked in the model's top 10 — was pre-specified as the primary metric.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-10] Uncertainty quantification used a cluster bootstrap resampling whole origins with replacement (10,000 resamples).[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-11] Two further robustness checks were applied: leave-one-origin-out and a non-overlapping-origin subset of 4 origins spaced exactly 14 days apart.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-12]

**Exploratory growth-rate extension for already-affected zones:** A secondary exploratory analysis examined near-term case growth for the 47 already-affected zones using a hierarchical negative-binomial growth-rate model, after an initial Poisson specification was found miscalibrated on rolling-origin backtesting.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-13] This is not presented as a second validated model of comparable standing, and a combined invasion-plus-growth ranking is not presented as a validated decision tool, as that combined ranking has not itself been backtested.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-14]

### Assumptions

**FOI kernel parameters:** The FOI term uses a distance-decay kernel K(z,z') = exp(−d(z,z')/φ) with φ = 60 minutes as the default decay scale, and a 3-day trailing mean of new confirmed cases through day t−2 as the incidence weight.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-19] The decay scale and smoothing window were not tuned against performance; across a grid of 8 alternative combinations (φ ∈ {30, 60, 120} minutes × window ∈ {1, 3, 7} days), the resulting zone ranking correlated with the primary specification at Spearman ρ = 0.90–0.99, with top-10 overlap of 9 or 10 out of 10 zones in every combination, indicating the ranking is stable across plausible kernel choices.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-5]

**Temporal lag to avoid leakage:** Same-day leakage is addressed by using only incidence through day t−2, never day t or t−1, to construct the FOI covariate.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-20] An earlier, same-day (non-lagged) specification yielded a FOI coefficient of +0.610 [0.43, 0.79] compared to +0.357 [0.16, 0.55] after applying the lag, indicating same-day leakage had inflated the apparent effect by roughly 40%.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-6]

**Outcome definition:** The outcome modelled is a zone's first reported confirmed case, not the date of biological introduction of the virus; first confirmation reflects viral introduction, local transmission, detection, testing access, reporting capacity, and retrospective case reclassification jointly, and the model cannot separate these.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-21]

**Static covariates and structural form:** Health-facility density (β = +0.486, [0.245, 0.734]) and population (β = +0.400, [0.087, 0.720]) are associated with hazard at the 95% credible level and are included as structural inputs; socioeconomic deprivation (+0.326, [−0.074, 0.754]) and province-level conflict exposure (+0.156, [−0.640, 0.900]) were not distinguishable from zero.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-7] Conflict exposure is measured only at province level across four provinces, alongside a province random effect estimated from the same four groups; this covariate's estimate is described as exploratory.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-4-discussion-2]

**Data-vintage leakage and pseudo-prospective evaluation:** Data-vintage leakage — whether case counts available for a historical date already reflect corrections made after that date — cannot be ruled out, since only the final, most recently corrected dataset is held rather than dated historical snapshots; the rolling-origin evaluation is therefore described as pseudo-prospective throughout, not a true contemporaneous test.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-22]

**Province random effect:** A non-centred province random effect is included in the linear predictor alongside a piecewise-constant weekly baseline evolving as a random walk and weakly informative priors.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-23]

### Limitations

**Pseudo-prospective evaluation and data-vintage leakage:** Because historical snapshots of the surveillance dataset at each rolling-origin date were not available, the authors cannot rule out that retrospective data revision is reflected in what the evaluation treats as contemporaneously known information; the rolling-origin evaluation is therefore described as pseudo-prospective throughout, reserving the term prospective for the genuinely pre-registered future verification.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-1-introduction-2]

**Outcome reflects detection as well as introduction:** The outcome modelled is first reported confirmed case, not the unobserved date of viral introduction; these differ to the extent that introduction, local transmission, detection, testing access, and reporting capacity differ across zones, and the model cannot separate these mechanisms. This matters specifically for the health-facility-density covariate, whose positive association with hazard may partly or wholly reflect greater detection capacity in better-served zones rather than, or in addition to, genuinely greater introduction risk.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-4-discussion-3]

**Absence of mobile-phone mobility data:** Existing approaches to spatial spread typically rely on mobile-phone call detail record data, which a systematic review found available in only a small number of African countries, not including DRC; CDR data were therefore not used in this analysis.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-1-introduction-3][^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-6]

**Statistical uncertainty in the primary comparison:** The top-10 hit rate comparison against road-distance reaches only the boundary of conventional significance (CI lower bound 0.000); the authors state they would not describe this as a robust result on its own and rely on convergent evidence across the primary metric, the sensitivity grid, and the leave-one-origin-out and non-overlapping-origin checks collectively.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-4-discussion-4]

**AUC-PR divergence:** On AUC-PR, the baseline model with no connectivity term performed as well as or better than every connectivity specification; the authors report this plainly, noting that a global discrimination metric rewards correctly ranking the bulk of zones that will not invade soon, which static covariates alone appear to do reasonably well, while the connectivity terms' distinguishable contribution is specifically to sharpening the top of the ranking.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-8]

**Conflict exposure measured only at province level:** Conflict exposure is measured only at province level across four provinces, alongside a province random effect estimated from the same four groups; the authors state this covariate's estimate should be read as exploratory.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-4-discussion-2]

**Growth-rate extension not combined with hazard model:** The growth-rate extension has not been backtested as a combined ranking with the hazard model, and the authors do not present one for that reason.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-4-discussion-5]

**Fixed modelling choices not evaluated:** Fixed choices not evaluated in this analysis include the OSRM travel-time transformation and the province-level (rather than zone-level) conflict aggregation.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-4-discussion-6]

### Data sources

**Primary outbreak data:** Daily case data come from the INRB-UMIE BDBV2026-Data repository (Mbulayi et al., 2026).[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-15] The study period covers 14 May to 26 July 2026, yielding 5,727 zone-day observations and 47 first-case events across the risk set.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-1][^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-16]

**Auxiliary data:** Static covariates include WorldPop population estimates, OpenStreetMap/HOT health-facility points, Complex Crisis Vulnerability Index, and province-level ACLED conflict events.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-17] Road-network travel times were derived from a national OSRM graph.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-18] CDR/mobility data were not used, as DRC is not among the countries for which such data are available.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-6] An independent Flowminder-based mobility analysis (Bangelesa et al., 2026, as summarised in Walekhwa et al., 2026) found the outbreak's three epicentre zones among the top 5% of DRC health zones nationally on mobility intensity; this was used as external, indirect support for the general connectivity premise, not as a model input.[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-4-discussion-1]
:::

:::{tab-item} Li et al. (11 Aug 2026)
### Li et al. (11 Aug 2026)<br>Dynamics, Optimal Control, and Spillover Risk of the 2026 Bundibugyo Ebola Outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo

[Read the analysis](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text)

### Overview

This analysis develops a compartmental transmission model (SEIDR: susceptible-exposed-infectious-deceased-recovered) to characterize the early transmission dynamics of the 2026 BDBV outbreak in the DRC and to estimate the risk that the outbreak spreads internationally through population mobility. The model incorporates three categories of interventions—public self-protection, safe burial, and treatment and convalescence—to assess early transmission dynamics, the current epidemic trajectory, and cross-border spillover risk, and to inform the formulation of control strategies.[^li-2026-abstract-1] Spillover risk is separately estimated using International Migrant Stock data and Facebook IP-based mobility data with the Prophet forecasting model[^li-2026-abstract-2], and the analysis reports that four countries were identified as very high risk at the end of July[^li-2026-abstract-3], a number the authors project would grow substantially under a "status quo" scenario but stay comparatively contained under an optimized control strategy.

### Estimate

The paper's headline model output is not an outbreak-size estimate in isolation but a set of counterfactual/projected cumulative infection trajectories tied to control scenarios. Under the fitted (actual) trajectory, cumulative confirmed cases increased from 33 to 3,674[^li-2026-results-5] during the observation period (18 May–31 July). Had the derived optimal control strategy been applied from the estimated transmission start date, the simulated cumulative number of infections as of 31 July would have been approximately 135 cases, representing a 96.33% reduction compared with the 3,674 cases actually reported during the same period[^li-2026-results-6]. Projecting forward from the actual 31 July epidemic size under the optimal control strategy, cumulative infections would reach 4,372 (95% CI: 4,300–4,450) by 31 August and 4,397 (95% CI: 4,325–4,474) by 30 September, after which the epidemic would enter a plateau phase, with only one additional case by 31 October[^li-2026-results-7]. By contrast, under the status quo control scenario, the epidemic was projected to continue rising until the end of April 2027[^li-2026-results-8]. The basic reproduction number, a related transmission-intensity estimate, was ℛ0 = 1.83 (95% CI: 1.81–1.84), of which ℛ0I = 1.14 (62.18%) and ℛ0D = 0.69 (37.82%)[^li-2026-results-2].

### Methods

**SEIDR compartmental transmission model:** Building upon the classical SEIR framework, and incorporating the specific transmission characteristics of Ebola virus and the public health realities in the affected region, the model was extended to include a deceased compartment (D), resulting in a SEIDR structure[^li-2026-methods-1]. It was fitted to DRC national cumulative confirmed case counts to estimate the reproduction number and its living-case versus corpse-mediated components, and to infer the start date of sustained transmission.

**NSGA-II combined with Pontryagin's Minimum Principle (optimal control):** To overcome shortcomings of conventional PMP-based indirect methods, the authors propose a hybrid solution strategy combining PMP with non-dominated sorting genetic algorithm II (NSGA-II), using a global optimisation algorithm to generate high-quality initial guesses that guide PMP iterations towards rapid convergence to the globally optimal solution.[^li-2026-methods-2] This produces a time-varying optimal control strategy, with adjustments every two weeks[^li-2026-abstract-5], applied to the DRC-level SEIDR model.

**Migration-based spillover/importation risk model:** To assess the risk of Ebola virus disease spreading beyond the outbreak country (DRC) to other regions, international migration data were incorporated to evaluate cross-border spread risk, assuming homogeneous mixing and equal probability of out-migration, with the number of imported cases modelled as following a binomial distribution based on cumulative out-migrants, cumulative confirmed cases in the DRC, and total DRC population.[^li-2026-methods-3] Annual International Migrant Stock data served as the baseline measure of population connectivity, with monthly outflow proportions estimated using Facebook IP-address-based migration data for 2019–2022 and a Prophet forecasting model to capture seasonal patterns and trend changes.[^li-2026-methods-4] This method produces country-level importation risk estimates and rankings for destinations worldwide, run separately under status-quo and optimized-control scenarios for August–December 2026.

### Assumptions

**Start date of sustained transmission:** the model treats the transmission onset as unknown and searched over a range; the possible start date of sustained transmission was set to range between 24 March and 24 April, generating 32 candidate start-date scenarios[^li-2026-results-10], with 28 March identified as the optimal fitting start date (R²=0.7586), and start dates between 31 March and 3 April also yielding good fitting performance (R²>0.7)[^li-2026-results-11].

**Homogeneous mixing and national-population susceptible pool:** the model assumes homogeneous mixing of the population in the affected country and equal probability of out-migration for all individuals[^li-2026-methods-9], and uses the total national population of the DRC as the susceptible population base, without accounting for geographic clustering of the outbreak or the effect of population mobility on contact patterns[^li-2026-discussion-1].

**Worst-case importation assumption:** the use of cumulative cases rather than active infections represents a worst-case scenario assumption, in which all reported infected individuals are considered potentially infectious[^li-2026-methods-10], driving the binomial importation-probability calculation.

**Intervention parameters (θ, ρ, μ):** the model's control levers are calibrated with fitted values of public self-protection (θ) fitted at 0.2332, safe burial rate (ρ) fitted at 0.2664, and treatment/recovery rate (μ) fitted at 0.3227[^li-2026-results-12], with thresholds identified where ℛ0 fell below 1 when θ exceeded 58.12%, and ℛ0I < 1 when θ exceeded 32.65%[^li-2026-results-13], ℛ0 < 1 only when μ > 98.46% but ℛ0I < 1 when μ > 43.31%[^li-2026-results-14], and ℛ0D < 1 when ρ exceeded 20.09%[^li-2026-results-15]. Sensitivity ranges for these parameters were constrained: the ranges for θ and μ were set to [0, 1], and the range for ρ was set to [0.1, 1][^li-2026-results-16], because ℛ0D is undefined when ρ = 0.

**Risk-tier thresholds:** country-level spillover classification is based on the normal distribution of importation risk probabilities as of end-July, stratified into four levels: 0 < Pb,t < 16.38% as low risk, 16.38% ≤ Pb,t < 43.68% as moderate risk, 43.68% ≤ Pb,t < 70.98% as high risk, and 70.98% ≤ Pb,t < 1 as very high risk[^li-2026-results-17].

### Limitations

**National-level population base obscures spatial and mobility effects on transmission:** the model used the total national population of the DRC as the susceptible population base, without accounting for geographic clustering of the outbreak or the effect of population mobility on contact patterns, which may lead to systematic underestimation of the transmission rate parameter β.[^li-2026-discussion-1]

**Optimal control strategy is theoretical and unvalidated operationally:** although the optimised control functions provide mathematically derived theoretical optima, their operational effectiveness and practical implementation in the actual outbreak context require further validation.[^li-2026-discussion-3]

### Data sources

Primary outbreak data: daily cumulative confirmed case counts for the DRC were extracted from WHO's Daily epidemiological update on acute public health events: Ebola disease—Bundibugyo virus, compiled by COUSP-DRC, retrieved as a complete time series from the start of the outbreak up to the study cut-off date (31 July 2026)[^li-2026-methods-5]. Background totals cite 4,566 confirmed cases and 2,128 deaths reported as of 11 August[^li-2026-abstract-4], and reported imported cases: Uganda has reported two imported cases with 20 cumulative confirmed cases domestically; France has reported one imported case with no local secondary transmission[^li-2026-results-9].

Auxiliary data: Population data were obtained from the World Population Prospects 2024, published by the United Nations Department of Economic and Social Affairs, Population Division, covering the total populations of the DRC and potential risk-importation countries[^li-2026-methods-6]. Cross-border mobility data were derived from the International Migrant Stock 2024: Destination and Origin, published by the United Nations Department of Economic and Social Affairs, with monthly allocation weights determined according to reference, to estimate the volume and spatial distribution of outflows from the DRC to neighbouring countries and major destination countries.[^li-2026-methods-7] Monthly out-migration data from the DRC based on Facebook IP addresses for the period 2019–2022[^li-2026-methods-8] were used with the Prophet model. Historical outbreak parameters were also drawn from the literature: the 2007 outbreak in Uganda reported 116 confirmed cases with 39 deaths, with a basic reproduction number estimated at 1.53; the 2012 outbreak in the DRC reported 77 cases.[^li-2026-discussion-2]
:::

:::{tab-item} Klein et al. (2 Aug 2026)
### Klein et al. (2 Aug 2026)<br>Model-guided placement of decentralized diagnostics for spatial control of the 2026 Bundibugyo Ebola epidemic

[Read the analysis](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text)

### Overview

The analysis estimates how many future deaths and new health-zone invasions in DRC could be averted by placing decentralized PCR laboratories and postmortem rapid tests ahead of the outbreak frontier, rather than where cases are already concentrated. It fits a Bayesian spatial branching process to public situation reports through 2 August 2026, using Flowminder movement data and a latent-burden anchor.[^klein-2026-abstract-1] It then replays 2,000 posterior trees with 5-100 zone-level PCR laboratories, deployment delays of 0-180 days, and postmortem RDT coverage.[^klein-2026-abstract-2] Speed dominated: 35 PCR laboratories averted a median 49% of future deaths if deployed immediately, 38% after 14 days, and 11% after 60 days[^klein-2026-abstract-3]. With a 30-day delay, model-informed placement averted 18-33% of future deaths with 20-100 laboratories, versus 1-8% under random placement.[^klein-2026-abstract-4] All results are conditional on an assumed national response ramp, not a fitted one.

### Estimate

The analysis does not estimate current outbreak size as its headline. Its headline results are deaths averted under diagnostic scenarios. It does report a simulated status-quo projection, conditional on the assumed national response ramp. Under the status quo plus the selected national response ramp, the simulated outbreak reached a median of 31,615 cumulative infections, 14,256 deaths, and 110 invaded health zones by 540 days after 2 August 2026.[^klein-2026-3-results-2] No intervals are given for these medians. Of those deaths, a median 4,816 were already locked in by the calibration horizon, leaving a median 9,360 future deaths that could still be averted.[^klein-2026-3-results-3] The calibration matched 3,802 cumulative confirmed cases and 1,707 cumulative confirmed deaths at 2 August 2026.[^klein-2026-2-methods-1]

Intervention results, as a share of deaths still avertable after the cutoff, are as follows.

- With 35 PCR laboratories, a median 49% of future deaths were averted if deployed immediately, 38% after 14 days and 11% after 60 days, with 90% prediction intervals of 30-65%, 23-53% and 5-20%.[^klein-2026-abstract-3]
- With a 30-day delay, model-informed placement averted 18-33% of deaths with 20-100 laboratories, versus 1-8% under random placement.[^klein-2026-abstract-4]
- Postmortem RDTs raised future deaths averted from 26% to 41% with 20 laboratories, a 14-day delay and 75% coverage.[^klein-2026-abstract-5]

For spatial spread, the status quo seeded a median 32 new health zones after 2 August 2026, versus 29, 26 and 25 with 20, 50 and 100 model-placed laboratories.[^klein-2026-3-results-4]

### Methods

**Bayesian spatial branching process (status-quo calibration):** This is an individual-level stochastic model in which each infection has a parent, a health zone, and detection, isolation and burial states. The epidemic was simulated as an individual-level stochastic branching process.[^klein-2026-2-methods-7] Each offspring infection stays in the local zone or jumps to another zone drawn from Flowminder outflows.[^klein-2026-2-methods-8] It was fitted to the INSP/INRB-UMIE situation-report time series for DRC nationally (health-zone level). It also used a latent-burden anchor from the EpiForecasts BVD-OutbreakSize analysis.[^klein-2026-2-methods-9] Calibration used a pseudo-marginal MCMC sampler.[^klein-2026-2-methods-10] It yields joint posterior samples of parameters and epidemic trajectories. A possible national reduction in transmission after the calibration horizon was added to represent broad behavioral change and non-diagnostic response.[^klein-2026-2-methods-11] This produced one plausible future at roughly West-Africa scale and was not a posterior estimate of future response strength.[^klein-2026-2-methods-12]

**Matched counterfactual replay of diagnostic scenarios:** The same posterior trees were replayed with and without new PCR laboratories and postmortem RDTs. Each intervention scenario prunes only the transmissions that earlier detection, isolation, tracing or safe burial would have prevented. Both simulations shared the same per-case random numbers.[^klein-2026-2-methods-13] Budgets of 5 to 100 laboratories were placed in health zones not served by existing laboratories.[^klein-2026-2-methods-14] A lazy CELF optimizer selected laboratories by marginal deaths averted, with random placement as the comparator.[^klein-2026-2-methods-15] Deployment delays of 0, 14, 30, 60, 90, 120 and 180 days were tested.[^klein-2026-2-methods-3] RDT scenarios raised postmortem testing to 50% or 75% coverage, either inside the PCR footprint or everywhere.[^klein-2026-2-methods-16] A sample-transport sensitivity analysis let unserved zones ship specimens to the nearest new laboratory.[^klein-2026-2-methods-17]

### Assumptions

**National response ramp:** This is the assumption the results depend on most. A national reduction in transmission after 2 August 2026 stands in for broad behavioral change and non-diagnostic response.[^klein-2026-2-methods-11] It was chosen against a 540-day West Africa anchor. The primary scenario reached half its effect after 90 days, with a final scale of 0.25 and Hill exponent 2.[^klein-2026-2-methods-21][^klein-2026-2-methods-22] It is chosen rather than fitted because the likelihood is blind to its parameters inside the fitted window.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-3] The authors state that much faster non-diagnostic control would leave fewer deaths to avert, while larger or more sustained transmission would widen the PCR placement window.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-4] They expect the relative ranking of strategies to be less sensitive than the absolute benefit.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-5]

**Natural history and transmission:** The generation interval is gamma with mean 15.3 days and SD 9.3 days.[^klein-2026-2-methods-19] The incubation period is lognormal with mean 6.3 days and SD 3.5 days.[^klein-2026-2-methods-20] Onset-to-death has a gamma distribution with mean 12 days, and onset-to-recovery a gamma with mean 16 days.[^klein-2026-2-methods-23] Also fixed is the assumption that 37% of a fatal case's transmission potential falls in a 0.8-day postmortem period. These were varied only in scenario sensitivity checks.[^klein-2026-2-methods-24] Offspring dispersion k is fixed at 0.372 after weak identification in earlier fits.[^klein-2026-appendix-1-key-parameters-1] The posterior median R0 was 2.63, the median fitted infection fatality ratio 0.46, and the latest Rt 1.11.[^klein-2026-3-results-5]

**Spatial movement:** Between-zone jumps use a single fitted jump probability shared across zones, with Flowminder flows setting relative destination probabilities.[^klein-2026-2-methods-25] The median jump probability was 0.19.[^klein-2026-3-results-6] The model places cases where infection occurred and has no explicit referral to regional hospitals, nosocomial amplification, or facility-level transmission.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-6] Susceptible depletion is ignored, which the authors call reasonable at the fitted attack rates but a limitation for very prolonged or very large trajectories.[^klein-2026-2-methods-26]

**Laboratory catchment and diagnosis:** The primary analysis assumed routine sample transport between health zones is highly challenging, so a laboratory primarily serves its own zone.[^klein-2026-2-methods-27] Zones with existing laboratories were excluded from new placement, leaving 462 candidates.[^klein-2026-2-methods-28] A sensitivity analysis lets unserved zones ship specimens to the nearest new laboratory.[^klein-2026-2-methods-17] PCR sensitivity is not modelled as a separate failure process; a sampled true infection is assumed PCR-confirmed.[^klein-2026-2-methods-29] Tracing is an offspring-level probability rather than an explicit contact graph.[^klein-2026-2-methods-30] Tracing from a PCR-confirmed index was inferred to intercept a median 69% of its offspring.[^klein-2026-3-results-7]

**Postmortem RDT and care-seeking:** RDT scenarios raise postmortem testing to at least 50% or 75% coverage of otherwise undiagnosed deaths and assume 80% sensitivity.[^klein-2026-2-methods-31] A positive RDT is assumed to lead to safe burial without additional modeled delay.[^klein-2026-2-methods-32] The scenarios assume demand for decentralized care. The authors say limited care seeking would weaken the mechanism.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-7][^klein-2026-4-discussion-8]

### Limitations

**Stylized model structure:** The authors describe the model as intentionally stylized. It ignores susceptible depletion, omits onward transmission after importations to neighboring countries, represents contact tracing as a probability on true offspring, and has no healthcare-facility compartment or nosocomial channel.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-9] These choices soften facility-level siting conclusions. The model should be read as ranking broad frontier risk and mechanism sizes, not as a deterministic list of which clinics must receive devices.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-10]

**Assumed rather than fitted response ramp:** The national response ramp is chosen, not fitted, so all diagnostic effects are marginal to it.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-11] The size of the baseline future affects the numerical scale of every counterfactual.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-12] The authors state that the exact timing of the benefit decline is tied to the assumed future response trajectory.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-13]

**Catchment and transport assumptions:** The intervention analysis was limited to one primary sample-transport assumption.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-14] If a zone assigned to an existing laboratory is not served quickly in practice, the model would wrongly give it a high PCR probability and conclude a new laboratory there has little value.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-15]

**Fixed-allocation and national objective:** The placement result is a fixed allocation problem, with the optimizer given the posterior at one decision time.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-16] Field placement is more reactive.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-17] The objective minimized burden inside DRC only.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-2]

**RDT pathways and cost not represented:** Faster notification from postmortem positives back to local awareness is not represented, so RDT effects are one-sided lower bounds for any pathway that would also accelerate recognition or tracing.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-18] The model considers epidemiologic benefit but not cost, so it cannot determine whether a given mix of laboratories, staff, transport and burial teams is cost-effective.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-19]

**Care-seeking demand:** Limited care seeking would weaken the mechanism by which closer diagnostics find cases earlier.[^klein-2026-4-discussion-8]

### Data sources

**Primary outbreak data:** The public INSP/INRB-UMIE record contained 3,802 confirmed cases and 1,707 confirmed deaths across 51 health zones by 2 August 2026.[^klein-2026-1-introduction-1] The data were pinned locally at SitRep 81, with data to 3 August 2026.[^klein-2026-2-methods-2] Laboratory activation dates, approximate PCR turnaround tiers, early Bunia and Rwampara capacity, and Ituri PCR throughput anchors were compiled from the laboratory sections of public situation reports.[^klein-2026-2-methods-18]
