[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-1]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "3 Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "The top-ranked zone, Rethy (Ituri), had a posterior median daily hazard of 0.095 (95% CrI [0.018, 0.295]), corresponding to roughly a 75% chance of reporting a first case within 14 days if current conditions persisted; the 20th-ranked zone's daily hazard corresponds to roughly 4% over the same window."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-2]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "3 Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "On the pre-specified primary metric (top-10 hit rate), FOI was highest (42.6%), followed by OSRM-distance and gravity (both 32.8%), then baseline (29.5%)."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-3]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "3 Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "On AUC-PR, the secondary metric with the most divergent picture, the baseline model with no connectivity term at all performed as well as or better than every connectivity specification (baseline 0.437 vs. FOI 0.409, gravity 0.399, OSRM-distance 0.239), and FOI's advantage over baseline on this metric was not distinguishable from zero (−0.027, 95% CI [−0.274, 0.200])."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-4]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "3 Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Across a grid of 8 alternative combinations (φ ∈ {30,60,120} minutes × window ∈ {1,3,7} days), the resulting zone ranking correlated with the primary specification's ranking at Spearman ρ = 0.90-0.99, with top-10 overlap of 9 or 10 out of 10 zones in every combination tested."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-1-introduction-1]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "1 Introduction"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "This paper develops a discrete-time hazard model in which spatial connectivity is represented by the observed incidence in every currently affected zone, propagated to at-risk zones through a road-network distance-decay kernel, rather than by geometric distance to the nearest affected zone alone. We refer to this as an incidence-weighted spatial force-of-infection (FOI) hazard model."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-5]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Daily case data from the INRB-UMIE BDBV2026-Data repository (Mbulayi et al., 2026). Static covariates: WorldPop population estimates; OpenStreetMap/HOT health-facility points; Complex Crisis Vulnerability Index; province-level ACLED conflict events (Raleigh et al., 2010). Road-network travel times from a national OSRM graph."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-6]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "CDR/mobility data were not used, as DRC is not among the countries for which this is available (Wardle et al., 2023)."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-7]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "The FOI kernel's decay scale (φ, fixed at 60 minutes in the primary specification) and smoothing window (3 days) were not tuned against performance; we instead report ranking stability across a grid of plausible alternatives, refitting the full model at each combination and comparing the resulting zone ranking to the primary specification via Spearman rank correlation and top-10 overlap: φ ∈ {30, 60, 120} minutes × window ∈ {1, 3, 7} days (8 combinations in addition to the primary specification)."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-8]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Four specifications of *x*z(*t*)'s connectivity term were compared, all embedded in the identical model described in Section 2.3, differing only in this one covariate: 1. Baseline: no connectivity term (four static covariates only). 2. Road-distance: log(1 + travel time to the nearest already-affected zone), the specification used in an earlier version of this analysis. 3. Gravity: a population-weighted score summed over all affected zones: - (iv) FOI (primary specification): incidence-weighted, kernel-summed force of infection,."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-9]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Fitting all four within the identical architecture, rather than comparing a fitted model against externally-computed rankings, isolates what each connectivity term contributes beyond the static covariates and beyond each other, and was added specifically in response to reviewer concern that an earlier version compared a fitted model to uncalibrated external scores."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-10]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Eight weekly origins, 21 May-9 July 2026, each refit using only data available through that origin, scored against zones reporting a first case within the following 14 days (Tashman, 2000). We designate top-10 hit rate - the proportion of true next-invading zones ranked in the model's top 10 at that origin - as the primary evaluation metric, specified before examining comparative results across specifications, because the operational decision this model is built to support is which small number of zones warrant prioritised attention, not discrimination across the full 59-zone risk set."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-11]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "We used a cluster (block) bootstrap resampling whole origins with replacement (10,000 resamples), for each replicate pooling the zone-level rows (or origin-level AUC-PR values) belonging to the resampled origin set and recomputing each metric as in the point estimate."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-12]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "We report two further checks: leave-one-origin-out (recomputing pooled metrics excluding each origin in turn, to assess whether any single origin drives the result), and a non-overlapping-origin subset (4 origins spaced exactly 14 days apart: 21 May, 4 June, 18 June, 2 July), which removes horizon overlap entirely at the cost of halving the usable origins and events (61 to 35)."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-13]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "A secondary, exploratory analysis - not a second validated model of comparable standing to the hazard model above - examined near-term case growth for the 47 zones already affected, using a hierarchical negative-binomial growth-rate model (an initial Poisson specification was found miscalibrated on rolling-origin backtesting and corrected)."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-14]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "We summarise the headline finding here (Section 3.5) and do not present a combined invasion-plus-growth ranking as a validated decision tool in the main paper, since that combined ranking has not itself been backtested."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-19]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "with kernel *K*(z,z') = exp(−*d*(z,z')/*φ*), *φ* = 60 minutes (default), and *C*z'(*t*) the 3-day trailing mean of new confirmed cases in zone *z'* through day *t* − 2 (Section 2.4)."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-5]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "3 Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "The FOI kernel's decay scale and smoothing window were not tuned against performance. Across a grid of 8 alternative combinations (φ ∈ {30,60,120} minutes × window ∈ {1,3,7} days), the resulting zone ranking correlated with the primary specification's ranking at Spearman ρ = 0.90-0.99, with top-10 overlap of 9 or 10 out of 10 zones in every combination tested."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-20]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Same-day leakage - using zone z's incidence recorded on day t to predict zone z's outcome on day t - is addressed directly: the FOI covariate (Section 2.5) uses only incidence through day t−2, never day t or t−1."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-6]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "3 Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "This is smaller than an earlier, same-day (non-lagged) specification's estimate of +0.610 [0.43, 0.79]; fixing same-day information leakage (Section 2.4) reduced the coefficient's magnitude by roughly 40%, confirming the leakage had inflated the apparent effect, though the corrected estimate still excludes zero."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-21]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "The outcome modelled is a zone's first reported confirmed case, denoted *T*z, with *y*z(*t*) = 1 on the day this occurs. We use this precise language deliberately rather than 'invasion': first confirmation reflects viral introduction, local transmission, detection, testing access, reporting capacity, and retrospective case reclassification jointly, and the model cannot separate these."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-7]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "3 Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Health-facility density (β = +0.486, [0.245, 0.734]) and population (β = +0.400, [0.087, 0.720]) were also associated with hazard at the 95% credible level; socioeconomic deprivation (+0.326, [−0.074, 0.754]) and province-level conflict exposure (+0.156, [−0.640, 0.900]) were not."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-4-discussion-2]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "4 Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Conflict exposure is measured only at province level across four provinces, alongside a province random effect estimated from the same four groups; this covariate's estimate should be read as exploratory."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-22]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Data-vintage leakage - whether the case counts available to us for a historical date t already reflect corrections made after t, since we hold only the final, most recently corrected version of the dataset rather than dated historical snapshots - cannot be ruled out with the data available to us. We flag this explicitly rather than assume it away, and for this reason describe the rolling-origin evaluation throughout as pseudo-prospective: a reconstruction of what a real-time analysis would have produced, run retrospectively on the best data now available, not a true contemporaneous test."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-23]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Linear predictor: with a piecewise-constant weekly baseline evolving as a random walk: a non-centred province random effect (Betancourt and Girolami, 2015): weakly informative priors: and the discrete-time survival log-likelihood, summed over zones and at-risk days:."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-1-introduction-2]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "1 Introduction"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Second, because historical snapshots of the surveillance dataset at each rolling-origin date were not available to us, we cannot rule out that some retrospective data revision (case reclassification, backfilled reports) is reflected in what our evaluation treats as contemporaneously known information. We therefore describe the rolling-origin evaluation as pseudo-prospective throughout, reserving the term prospective for the genuinely pre-registered future verification described in Section 2.10."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-4-discussion-3]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "4 Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "The outcome modelled throughout is first reported confirmed case, not the unobserved date of viral introduction. These differ to the extent that introduction, local transmission, detection, testing access, and reporting capacity differ across zones, and we cannot separate these mechanisms with the data available. This matters specifically for the health-facility-density covariate, whose positive association with hazard may partly or wholly reflect greater detection capacity in better-served zones rather than, or in addition to, genuinely greater introduction risk."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-1-introduction-3]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "1 Introduction"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "None of these efforts provides a validated, prospectively evaluated model of zone-level invasion risk specifically, and existing approaches that do address spatial spread typically rely on mobile-phone call detail record (CDR) data, which a systematic review found available in only a small number of African countries, not including the DRC (Wardle et al., 2023)."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-4-discussion-4]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "4 Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "The top-10-vs-OSRM-distance comparison reaches only the boundary of conventional significance (CI lower bound 0.000); we would not describe this as a robust result on its own and rely on the convergent evidence across the primary metric, the sensitivity grid, and the leave-one-origin-out and non-overlapping-origin checks collectively, rather than on any single comparison."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-3-results-8]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "3 Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "On AUC-PR, the secondary metric with the most divergent picture, the baseline model with no connectivity term at all performed as well as or better than every connectivity specification (baseline 0.437 vs. FOI 0.409, gravity 0.399, OSRM-distance 0.239), and FOI's advantage over baseline on this metric was not distinguishable from zero (−0.027, 95% CI [−0.274, 0.200]). We report this plainly rather than selectively: a global discrimination metric like AUC-PR rewards a model for correctly ranking the bulk of zones that will not invade soon, which the static covariates alone (population, facility density, deprivation) appear to do reasonably well without any connectivity information; the connectivity terms' distinguishable contribution is specifically to sharpening the top of the ranking, which is what the primary metric measures and what the operational use case (which few zones get attention first) requires."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-4-discussion-5]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "4 Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "The growth-rate extension (Section 3.5) has not been backtested as a combined ranking with the hazard model, and we do not present one for that reason."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-4-discussion-6]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "4 Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Fixed choices not evaluated here include the OSRM travel-time transformation and the province-level (rather than zone-level) conflict aggregation."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-15]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Daily case data from the INRB-UMIE BDBV2026-Data repository (Mbulayi et al., 2026)."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-1]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Retrospective, pseudo-prospective observational study of health-zone-level case emergence during the 2026 BDBV outbreak, 14 May-26 July 2026 (74 days)."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-16]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "This construction yields 5,727 zone-day observations, 47 events."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-17]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Static covariates: WorldPop population estimates; OpenStreetMap/HOT health-facility points; Complex Crisis Vulnerability Index; province-level ACLED conflict events (Raleigh et al., 2010)."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026a-2-materials-and-methods-18]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026a) "2 Materials and Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.12.26360244.full-text): "Road-network travel times from a national OSRM graph."

[^li-2026-abstract-2]: [Li et al. (2026) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "Using International Migrant Stock data and Facebook IP-based mobility data with the Prophet forecasting model, we assessed spillover risk."

[^li-2026-results-6]: [Li et al. [^li-2026-results-7]: [Li et al. [^li-2026-results-8]: [Li et al. (2026) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "By contrast, under the status quo control scenario, the epidemic was projected to continue rising until the end of April 2027 (supplementary Fig. S6)."
[^li-2026-results-2]: [Li et al. (2026) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "Based on parameter estimation using cumulative confirmed case data up to 30 June 2026, with parameters estimated via Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) methods, the basic reproduction number for this outbreak was estimated as ℛ0 = 1.83 (95% CI: 1.81–1.84), of which ℛ0*I* = 1.14 (62.18%) and ℛ0*D* = 0.69 (37.82%)."
[^li-2026-methods-1]: [Li et al. (2026) "Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "Building upon the classical SEIR framework, and incorporating the specific transmission characteristics of Ebola virus and the public health realities in the affected region, we extended the model to include a deceased compartment (D), resulting in a SEIDR structure (supplementary Fig. S1)."
[^li-2026-methods-2]: [Li et al. (2026) "Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "To overcome these shortcomings, we propose a hybrid solution strategy combining PMP with non-dominated sorting genetic algorithm II (NSGA-II). The core principle is to use a global optimisation algorithm to generate high-quality initial guesses, thereby guiding PMP iterations towards rapid convergence to the globally optimal solution."
[^li-2026-abstract-5]: [Li et al. (2026) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "By integrating the non-dominated sorting genetic algorithm II (NSGA-II) with Pontryagin's minimum principle (PMP), we derived a time-varying optimal control strategy, with adjustments every two weeks, that could shorten the epidemic duration by approximately 7 months."
[^li-2026-methods-3]: [Li et al. (2026) "Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "To assess the risk of Ebola virus disease spreading beyond the outbreak country (DRC) to other regions, this study incorporated international migration data to evaluate the risk of cross-border spread via population connectivity from the perspective of transnational migration. Assuming homogeneous mixing of the population in the affected country and equal probability of out-migration for all individuals, the number of imported cases from affected country *e* to destination country *b* by month *t* was assumed to follow a binomial distribution: where *T*e,b,t is the cumulative number of out-migrants from affected country *e* to destination country *b* from epidemic onset to month *t*, *C*e,t is the cumulative number of confirmed cases in affected country *e* as of month *t*, and *N*e is the total population of affected country *e*."
[^li-2026-methods-4]: [Li et al. (2026) "Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "We used the annual International Migrant Stock as a baseline measure of population connectivity strength. To further estimate the monthly proportion of outflows from the DRC to neighbouring and major destination countries in 2026, we referred to reference 25, which provided monthly out-migration data from the DRC based on Facebook IP addresses for the period 2019–2022. We applied the Prophet forecasting model developed by Facebook Meta to capture inherent annual seasonal patterns in migration behaviour and automatically detect trend change points."
[^li-2026-results-10]: [Li et al. (2026) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "To investigate the timing of the transition from sporadic cases to sustained transmission, we set the possible start date of sustained transmission to range between 24 March and 24 April, generating 32 candidate start-date scenarios for sustained transmission, each of which was subjected to model fitting."
[^li-2026-results-11]: [Li et al. (2026) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "Across all 32 candidate start dates for sustained transmission, 28 March was identified as the optimal fitting start date of sustained transmission (Fig. 1a), with an R² of 0.7586, indicating good model fit. Start dates between 31 March and 3 April also yielded good fitting performance, with R² values exceeding 0.7 and ranking among the top across all evaluation metrics (Fig. 1b–e)."
[^li-2026-methods-9]: [Li et al. (2026) "Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "Assuming homogeneous mixing of the population in the affected country and equal probability of out-migration for all individuals, the number of imported cases from affected country *e* to destination country *b* by month *t* was assumed to follow a binomial distribution: where *T*e,b,t is the cumulative number of out-migrants from affected country *e* to destination country *b* from epidemic onset to month *t*, *C*e,t is the cumulative number of confirmed cases in affected country *e* as of month *t*, and *N*e is the total population of affected country *e*."
[^li-2026-discussion-1]: [Li et al. (2026) "Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "The model used the total national population of the DRC as the susceptible population base, without accounting for geographic clustering of the outbreak or the effect of population mobility on contact patterns, which may lead to systematic underestimation of the transmission rate parameter *β*."
[^li-2026-results-12]: [Li et al. (2026) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "| *θ* | Public self-protection | Community volunteers and primary health-care workers conducted door-to-door education on transmission routes, early symptom recognition, timely care-seeking, and risk reporting. Community and religious leaders were engaged to counter misinformation and improve adherence to protective measures. | 0.2332 | *θ*∗ = min{1, max{*p*, 0}} | | *ρ* | Safe burial of deceased bodies | Trained teams performed safe and dignified burials, including oral swab sampling, sealed cadaver bags, and chlorinated disinfection. Transparent-view bags were used for family identification, with community acceptance negotiated through religious leaders. | 0.2664 | *ρ*∗ = min{1, max{*q*, 0}} | | *μ* | Treatment and recovery of infected individuals | Treatment centres provided early rehydration and supportive care. In the absence of specific vaccines or therapeutics, international medical aid supplemented supplies and staffing, alongside infection prevention and control training for health-care workers. | 0.3227 | *μ*∗ = min{1, max{*m*, 0}} |."
[^li-2026-results-13]: [Li et al. (2026) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "When *θ* exceeded 58.12%, ℛ0 fell below 1. The suppressive effect of *θ* on human-to-human transmission was more pronounced, with ℛ0*I* < 1 when *θ* > 32.65%, indicating that this measure can effectively interrupt transmission chains driven by infected individuals (Fig. 2a)."
[^li-2026-results-14]: [Li et al. (2026) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "For pharmaceutical interventions (*μ*), the effect on the deceased-body-mediated transmission pathway was relatively weak, with ℛ0 < 1 achieved only when *μ* > 98.46%. However, *μ* exerted a more substantial effect on interrupting human-to-human transmission, with ℛ0*I* < 1 when *μ* > 43.31%, suggesting that improving treatment coverage even in resource-limited settings can effectively reduce transmission intensity in the population (Fig. 2b)."
[^li-2026-results-15]: [Li et al. (2026) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "By contrast, increasing the safe burial rate (*ρ*) had a limited effect on ℛ0*I* and overall ℛ0, but substantially reduced secondary transmission resulting from unsafe burials; when *ρ* > 20.09%, ℛ0*D* < 1, indicating that safe burial practices can effectively eliminate the contribution of the deceased pathway to epidemic amplification (Fig. 2c)."
[^li-2026-results-16]: [Li et al. (2026) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "Given that *ρ* appears in the denominator of the expression for ℛ0*D*, where the component is undefined when *ρ* = 0, in subsequent sensitivity analyses, the ranges for *θ* and *μ* were set to [0, 1], and the range for *ρ* was set to [0.1, 1]."
[^li-2026-results-17]: [Li et al. (2026) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "Based on the normal distribution of importation risk probabilities as of end-July, risk was stratified into four levels: 0 < *P*b,t < 16.38% as low risk, 16.38% ≤ *P*b,t < 43.68% as moderate risk, 43.68% ≤ *P*b,t < 70.98% as high risk, and 70.98% ≤ *P*b,t < 1 as very high risk (supplementary Fig. S4)."
[^li-2026-discussion-3]: [Li et al. (2026) "Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "Although the optimised control functions provide mathematically derived theoretical optima, their operational effectiveness and practical implementation in the actual outbreak context require further validation."
[^li-2026-methods-5]: [Li et al. (2026) "Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "Daily cumulative confirmed case counts for the DRC were extracted from WHO's *Daily epidemiological update on acute public health events: Ebola disease—Bundibugyo virus*, compiled and provided by the Democratic Republic of the Congo Public Health Emergency Operations Centre (COUSP-DRC). We retrieved the complete time-series data from the start of the outbreak up to the study cut-off date (31 July 2026)."
[^li-2026-abstract-4]: [Li et al. (2026) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "In 2026, a Bundibugyo ebolavirus (BDBV) outbreak emerged in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), with 4,566 confirmed cases and 2,128 deaths reported as of 11 August, potentially becoming the largest Ebola outbreak on record globally."
[^li-2026-results-9]: [Li et al. (2026) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "According to reported data, Uganda has reported two imported cases with 20 cumulative confirmed cases domestically; France has reported one imported case with no local secondary transmission."
[^li-2026-methods-6]: [Li et al. (2026) "Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "Population data were obtained from the *World Population Prospects 2024*, published by the United Nations Department of Economic and Social Affairs, Population Division, covering the total populations of the DRC and potential risk-importation countries."
[^li-2026-methods-7]: [Li et al. (2026) "Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "Cross-border mobility data were derived from the *International Migrant Stock 2024: Destination and Origin*, published by the United Nations Department of Economic and Social Affairs, with monthly allocation weights determined according to reference, to estimate the volume and spatial distribution of outflows from the DRC to neighbouring countries and major destination countries."
[^li-2026-methods-8]: [Li et al. (2026) "Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "To further estimate the monthly proportion of outflows from the DRC to neighbouring and major destination countries in 2026, we referred to reference 25, which provided monthly out-migration data from the DRC based on Facebook IP addresses for the period 2019–2022."
[^li-2026-discussion-2]: [Li et al. (2026) "Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.17.26360567.full-text): "The 2007 outbreak in Uganda reported 116 confirmed cases with 39 deaths, with a basic reproduction number estimated at 1.53; the 2012 outbreak in the DRC reported 77 cases."
[^okingo-2026-body-2]: [Okingo, Iroungou, & Valdano (2026) "Body"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.06.11.26355237): "By contrast, high sustained-transmission potential was distributed across DRC, including Kinshasa, Kasaï-Oriental, Kongo-Central, Tshopo, Nord-Kivu, Haut-Katanga, Sud-Kivu, Kasaï, and Kasaï-Central (Fig. 1C,D). Notably, several are far from the current outbreak."
[^okingo-2026-body-8]: [Okingo, Iroungou, & Valdano (2026) "Body"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.06.11.26355237): "Introduction risk was highest in HZs close to the reported foci, specifically in Ituri, Nord-Kivu, and Haut-Uele, consistent with expected mobility patterns (Fig. 1A,B)."
[^okingo-2026-body-4]: [Okingo, Iroungou, & Valdano (2026) "Body"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.06.11.26355237): "Crossing the two rankings identified eight HZs that combined high introduction risk and high sustained-transmission potential (Fig. 1E, F): Binza Ozone and Binza Meteo in Kinshasa; Karisimbi in Nord-Kivu; Kadutu and Ibanda in Sud-Kivu; and Makiso-Kisangani, Kabondo, and Mangobo in Tshopo."
[^okingo-2026-body-5]: [Okingo, Iroungou, & Valdano (2026) "Body"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.06.11.26355237): "We used the method developed in Ref. 2  to estimate, for each DRC health zone (HZ), the potential for an introduced case to generate sustained transmission (hereafter, sustained-transmission potential)."
[^okingo-2026-body-6]: [Okingo, Iroungou, & Valdano (2026) "Body"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.06.11.26355237): "We used available population and mobility data to parameterize spatial transmission, testing a broad range of assumptions on disease transmissibility and on how local and between-community contact patterns can be inferred from these data."
[^okingo-2026-body-7]: [Okingo, Iroungou, & Valdano (2026) "Body"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.06.11.26355237): "We ranked HZs by sustained-transmission potential and compared them with introduction-risk ranks estimated from mobility data."
[^okingo-2026-body-10]: [Okingo, Iroungou, & Valdano (2026) "Body"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.06.11.26355237): "This requires estimating introduction risk and, more importantly, the potential for sustained onward transmission, despite limited data on human mobility, contact patterns, disease transmissibility, tracing and isolation capacity."
[^okingo-2026-body-3]: [Okingo, Iroungou, & Valdano (2026) "Body"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.06.11.26355237): "As of June 4, confirmed cases in Nord-Kivu and Sud-Kivu indicate that the spread has gone beyond the initial focus."
[^okingo-2026-figure-1-introduction-risk-and-sustained-transmission-1]: [Okingo, Iroungou, & Valdano (2026) "Figure 1: Introduction risk and sustained-transmission potential across health zones."](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.06.11.26355237): "Population data are from worldpop.org."
[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-1]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "No transmission model is used at this stage; the analysis describes where any exported case is most likely to be detected, conditional on at least one export occurring."
[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-2]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "**This analysis describes** ***conditional*** **ranking: it does not assert that an international export will occur, but, given that one occurs, indicates where it is most likely to be detected."
[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-3]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "The framework is unchanged from Reports #1 to #4 and #6: air-travel and short-scale commuting flows are combined into a mobility-only relative importation risk (RR), evaluated over a two-week horizon."
[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-5]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "All other neighbours (South Sudan, Rwanda, Tanzania, Burundi, Central African Republic, Zambia, Angola, Republic of Congo) are retained in the tail; South Sudan (Juba and Rumbek) then leads the tail with ~53 %, followed by Kenya (~12 %) and the Central African Republic (~7 %)."
[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-6]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "** The absolute probability of any single international export remains small."
[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-11]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "** Across the neighbouring countries, 99.0 % of the city-level RR mass sits in five cities: Kasese (UGA, 58.4 %), Pakuba (UGA, 28.6 %), Arua (UGA, 10.8 %), Juba (SSD, 0.9 %), and Rumbek (SSD, 0.15 %)."
[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-13]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "** After removing Uganda, Africa retains 86.3 % of the tail (led by South Sudan; then Kenya, the Central African Republic, Tanzania, Ethiopia, Rwanda), followed by Europe 5.2 %, the Middle East 4.8 %, Asia 2.6 %, and the Americas 0.9 %."
[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-14]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "Behind South Sudan the leading destinations are Kenya (12.0 %), the Central African Republic (6.9 %), the UAE (3.2 %), Tanzania (2.9 %), and Ethiopia (1.7 %); beyond the UAE, intercontinental destinations sit at roughly 1 % or less."
[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-15]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "They represent the share of international export probability that would land in each destination *if* an international export were to occur. They do not represent the absolute probability that an export will occur."
[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-3]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Methods Overview"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "The modeling approach follows the GLEAM (Global Epidemic and Mobility) computational framework [4,5]."
[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-4]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Methods Overview"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "For each potential destination Y, the model estimates the probability P(Y) that an infected individual originating in the affected area travels to Y, conditional on at least one exported case occurring. The output is a relative risk distribution across all candidate destinations, normalized so that values represent the share of importation probability assigned to each location."
[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-5]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Methods Overview"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "Global air travel patterns are derived from the International Air Transport Association (IATA) and the Official Airline Guide (OAG), providing passenger-level origin–destination flows between international airports. These data are complemented by short-scale commuting patterns capturing daily movements between adjacent subpopulations, representing the local spread of disease through routine human activity within and across health zones."
[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-6]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Methods Overview"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "This update splits the assessment into two views over the same underlying RR distribution. The first (Section 3) reports the city-level RR across DRC's neighbouring countries (before renormalisation), showing that Uganda alone accounts for 98.0 % of the non-DRC mass. The second (Sections 4 and 5) removes only Uganda and renormalises the remaining mass over 120 international destinations reachable by air."
[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-1]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Limitations & Assumptions"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "The values reported here are **conditional** on at least one international export occurring. They describe how international exportation probability is distributed across destinations *given* that an international export happens, not the absolute probability that any specific destination will receive a case."
[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-2]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Limitations & Assumptions"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "They describe the mobility geometry of where an exported case would likely land, given that one occurs within the next two weeks."
[^epistorm-2026a-neighbouring-country-cities-2]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Neighbouring-Country Cities"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "Uganda alone accounts for 98.0 % of the non-DRC mass and is the sole country removed from the tail in Sections 4 and 5. With DRC and Uganda removed and the remainder renormalised to sum to one, the tail is led by South Sudan: Juba carries 45.9 %, and Rumbek adds a further 7.6 %."
[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-3]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Limitations & Assumptions"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "The incubation period of Bundibugyo virus (2–21 days; average ~11 days) constrains the window within which an exported case could reasonably travel while infectious or pre-symptomatic."
[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-4]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Limitations & Assumptions"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "The mobility model does not incorporate sociodemographic attributes of travellers (age, economic status, occupation, or pre-existing medical conditions), any of which may modulate both exposure risk at origin and care-seeking behaviour at destination. Travel probability within a catchment area is treated as independent of these attributes and of specific location within the catchment."
[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-5]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Limitations & Assumptions"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "These estimates apply to traffic from the general population. The importation risk associated with repatriation flights, responder evacuations, or other ad-hoc operational movements is not part of this analysis."
[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-6]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Limitations & Assumptions"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "Case importations are modelled as statistically independent events. Real-world events involving multiple related cases (for example, a family cluster travelling together) are counted as a single importation event."
[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-7]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Limitations & Assumptions"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "To the extent that such clusters are common, the model will under-count the number of distinct exposure events while correctly counting the number of distinct geographic seedings."
[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-8]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Limitations & Assumptions"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "The model does not predict downstream transmission chains following an importation event: risk estimates reflect the probability of a single importation and do not account for the probability of sustained local transmission in the receiving location."
[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-9]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Limitations & Assumptions"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "This report uses the mobility framework only. No transmission-dynamics model is applied, and the RR values should not be read as forecasts of future case counts at any destination."
[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-16]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "- **Out of scope** Absolute likelihood of any international export; downstream local transmission; outbreak burden at source."
[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-17]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "The unconditional probability of international export remains small, and this analysis does not model country-level border-control measures, which would in particular affect absolute risk."
[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-10]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Limitations & Assumptions"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "Any such measure would in particular reduce the *absolute* probability of an international export; conditional destination ranking is less sensitive but still affected. The RR values here should be interpreted as the mobility-only baseline."
[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-11]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Limitations & Assumptions"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "The mobility model does not incorporate sociodemographic attributes of travellers (age, economic status, occupation, or pre-existing medical conditions), any of which may modulate both exposure risk at origin and care-seeking behaviour at destination."
[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-12]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Limitations & Assumptions"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "The importation risk associated with repatriation flights, responder evacuations, or other ad-hoc operational movements is not part of this analysis."
[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-13]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Limitations & Assumptions"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "Real-world events involving multiple related cases (for example, a family cluster travelling together) are counted as a single importation event. To the extent that such clusters are common, the model will under-count the number of distinct exposure events while correctly counting the number of distinct geographic seedings."
[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-14]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Limitations & Assumptions"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "Mobility data may not fully capture informal cross-border movement, which is substantial within the immediate cross-border region but is not the focus of the international-tail analysis in Sections 4 and 5."
[^epistorm-2026a-limitations-assumptions-15]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Limitations & Assumptions"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "The unconditional probability of international export remains small, and is further modulated by any border-control interventions not captured in the model."
[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-7]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "This report updates the international-dissemination assessment using the 7 October 2026 DRC data."
[^epistorm-2026a-executive-summary-8]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "- **Pathogen** Bundibugyo virus, second Ebola species; first isolated 2007, Uganda; CFR ≈ 48% in DRC as of 10 October 2026 (8,978 cases, 4,336 deaths)."
[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-7]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Methods Overview"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "Global air travel patterns are derived from the International Air Transport Association (IATA) and the Official Airline Guide (OAG), providing passenger-level origin–destination flows between international airports."
[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-8]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Methods Overview"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "These data are complemented by short-scale commuting patterns capturing daily movements between adjacent subpopulations, representing the local spread of disease through routine human activity within and across health zones."
[^epistorm-2026a-methods-overview-9]: [Epistorm (2026a) "Methods Overview"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report8RR.html): "- **Incubation** BVD: 2–21 days, average ~11 days."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-1]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "- **Quantifies** Transmission parameters, current transmission level and true burden of the BVD outbreak in DRC, and its course to 30 November 2026."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-2]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "This update re-estimates local transmission with a new set of simulations of the GLEAM care-pathway model [5]."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-3]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "It is fitted to the increase in reported cases between 31 July and 30 September 2026 (eight situation reports), allowing for a delay of about 5 days between symptom onset and report, and checked against the 10 October count, which was not used in the fit."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-4]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Three isolation scenarios are retained: 50 %, 60 % and 75 % of symptomatic cases isolated."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-5]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "1. **About 60 % isolation is the best-supported scenario."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-6]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "** The effective reproduction number has been close to 1 since August: 0.98 in August and 0.95 in October under 60 % isolation."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-7]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "4. **Projection to 31 October 2026.** Under 60 % isolation, 10,465 [9,433 – 11,835] reported cases and 15,225 [12,151 – 19,694] true symptomatic cases in DRC, with about 83 new symptomatic cases per day."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-8]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "- **Out of scope** International dissemination (see Report #8)."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-11]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "In the 60 % scenario, transmission in the early phase before the interventions is characterised by a posterior R₀ of 1.87 [1.69 – 2.09] and an empirical doubling time of 13.2 [9.8 – 17.6] days."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-13]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "** Fitted to data up to 30 September, the 60 % scenario gives 9,069 [8,531 – 9,727] reported cases on 10 October, against 8,978 observed."
[^epistorm-2026b-projections-1]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Projections"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Three weeks after the last situation report, the 60 % scenario projects 15,225 [12,151 – 19,694] true symptomatic cases in DRC by 31 October, of which 10,465 [9,433 – 11,835] are expected to be reported, and about 83 [47 – 150] new symptomatic cases per day (Table 1)."
[^epistorm-2026b-projections-2]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Projections"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Under 60 % isolation, reported cases are projected to reach 12,259 [10,446 – 15,059] by 30 November, corresponding to about 17,741 true symptomatic cases."
[^epistorm-2026b-two-stage-calibration-scenario-selection-and-posteriors-5]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Two-stage calibration, scenario selection and posteriors"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Although both quantities refer to the pre-intervention regime, the posterior of R₀ depends on the isolation scenario: it increases from 1.71 [1.57 – 1.87] at 50 % to 2.13 [1.88 – 2.36] at 75 %."
[^epistorm-2026b-effective-reproduction-number-1-june-30-1]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Effective reproduction number · 1 June – 30 November 2026"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Since then it has remained close to the epidemic threshold: in October the median is 1.00 under 50 %, 0.95 under 60 % and 0.86 under 75 % isolation."
[^epistorm-2026b-projections-3]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Projections"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "| Reported cumulative | 10,979 [9,786 – 12,219] | 10,465 [9,433 – 11,835] | 9,859 [8,948 – 11,084] |."
[^epistorm-2026b-projections-4]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Projections"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "| True cumulative | 16,493 [12,781 – 24,114] | 15,225 [12,151 – 19,694] | 12,941 [10,507 – 15,280] |."
[^epistorm-2026b-projections-5]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Projections"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "| Reported cumulative | 13,382 [11,132 – 15,950] | 12,259 [10,446 – 15,059] | 11,071 [9,528 – 13,442] |."
[^epistorm-2026b-projections-6]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Projections"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "| True cumulative | 19,728 [14,711 – 29,049] | 17,741 [13,821 – 23,352] | 14,399 [11,174 – 18,147] |."
[^epistorm-2026b-projections-7]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Projections"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The scenarios differ more in the true burden than in the reported counts: higher isolation goes with higher ascertainment, so the same reported counts correspond to fewer true cases (16,493 under 50 % and 12,941 under 75 % isolation)."
[^epistorm-2026b-projections-8]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Projections"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The three scenarios agree on the recent past and differ in direction: under 50 % isolation the number of reported cases per day stays roughly flat (about 80 on 31 October and 80 on 30 November), under 60 % it declines slowly (64 and 56) and under 75 % it declines faster (47 and 34)."
[^epistorm-2026b-projections-9]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Projections"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Reported cases are very likely to exceed 10,000 by 30 November under 50 % and 60 % isolation (> 99 % and 99 %) and likely under 75 % (82 %). Exceeding 20,000 reported cases is very unlikely in all three scenarios (< 1 %)."
[^epistorm-2026b-projections-10]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Projections"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The probability of 50 or more new cases per day is 94 % under 50 %, 79 % under 60 % and 36 % under 75 % isolation. Under none of the scenarios is transmission expected to end by 30 November."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-4]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The framework is GLEAM (Global Epidemic and Mobility Model) [5], an Africa-wide metapopulation network coupled by daily commuting matrices and origin-destination passenger flows from IATA Passenger Intelligence Services and OAG."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-5]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The compartmental structure follows a Legrand-like SEIHFR scheme [7,8]. To model the impact of isolation and safe burial protocols, it incorporates three potential outcomes for an infectious individual (Figure 8):."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-6]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Transmission is overdispersed: the number of secondary cases generated by an infectious individual is not assumed to be Poisson distributed. Individual transmissibility follows a negative-binomial offspring distribution with dispersion k = 0.1, so that a small fraction of cases generates most secondary infections (see Compartmental structure)."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-10]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The model now includes individual-level heterogeneity in transmissibility (overdispersion, k = 0.1) and a gradual isolation ramp of 1 percentage point per day after each area's intervention trigger."
[^epistorm-2026b-two-stage-calibration-scenario-selection-and-posteriors-2]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Two-stage calibration, scenario selection and posteriors"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Stage 1 selects trajectories consistent with the early cross-border onsets in Uganda and with early activity in Beni. Stage 2 fits the reported DRC counts."
[^epistorm-2026b-two-stage-calibration-scenario-selection-and-posteriors-3]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Two-stage calibration, scenario selection and posteriors"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Because cases detected before and around the May alert were partly added retrospectively, the cumulative level up to the first August report is not used as a target; the model is fitted to the **increase** in reported cases since 31 July at seven situation-report dates up to 30 September."
[^epistorm-2026b-two-stage-calibration-scenario-selection-and-posteriors-4]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Two-stage calibration, scenario selection and posteriors"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Six isolation scenarios (20 % to 75 %) were compared on the same data, using Bayes factors: the ratio of the probability of the data under each scenario to that under the best-supported one."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-7]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The tolerance of the ABC step (5 %) was chosen to retain good fits only; the scenario ranking is the same for tolerances between 3 % and 10 %."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-8]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "R_t is the Cori estimator on simulated true daily onsets with a 7-day sliding window and a gamma serial interval (mean 15.3 days, standard deviation 9.3 days) discretised over 1–60 days."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-18]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Each scenario sets a target isolation level Q_cap (50 %, 60 % or 75 %)."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-19]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Reporting of new cases is modelled as proportional to isolation, α(t) = c × Q(t), where Q(t) is the isolation rate of each trajectory. Every isolated case is assumed to be reported (c ≥ 1) and ascertainment cannot exceed 1 (c ≤ 1/Q_cap)."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-16]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Projections assume that each scenario's isolation level is maintained, that ascertainment continues to track isolation and that the reporting delay stays constant."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-20]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The level of the epidemic before 31 July is only indirectly constrained, so the overall ascertainment and the true number of cases carry wide intervals."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-21]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Each DRC basin starts its own ramp of 1 percentage point per day toward the target when its cumulative onsets first reach 80; Bunia keeps a fixed start on 20 May 2026, and Uganda is held at 80 % throughout."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-22]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Isolation and case-finding timings are applied homogeneously within each basin; real-world coverage varies by location and over time."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-23]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The delay from symptom onset to report is assumed to follow a gamma distribution with a mean of 5 days and a standard deviation of 3.5 days, a typical delay from symptom onset to admission to a hospital or ETC and consistent with the onset-to-HCF time of the routine care pathway in the model (5 days). The distribution is discretised to whole days and held constant over time."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-24]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Standard deviations of 2.5 and 5 days give the same results."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-25]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The delay from symptom onset to report is an assumption (gamma, mean 5 days) and is held constant; changes in the delay over time and batch updates of the reported counts are not modelled."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-26]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "| k | Overdispersion of individual transmissibility (negative binomial, 4 discrete classes) | 0.1 | assumed |."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-27]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "| R₀ split | Community : HCF : Funeral at baseline | 0.44 : 0.22 : 0.34 | [8] |."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-28]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "| κ_iso | HCF iso transmission factor (β_{H,iso} / β_H) | 0.1 | assumed (residual nosocomial) |."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-29]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "| T_E | Latent period (Erlang-2 mean), sampled | U(5, 10) d | [8, 9] |."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-30]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "| T_iso | Onset to HCF admission, iso track | 2 d | [6] |."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-31]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The epidemic originates in Ituri, DRC, with 5 infectious cases on the inferred start date."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-32]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "**Stage 1** samples R₀ ~ U(1.2, 4.0) and the outbreak start date ~ U(1 February, 15 March 2026) and keeps trajectories consistent with the early epidemiological evidence."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-33]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Symptomatic individuals: 50 % of community and community-hospital-bound cases remain mobile (health-care-seeking travel and continued commuting), the other 50 % stay in their home basin; isolated (I_iso), hospitalised (H, H_ETC) and ETC compartments do not travel."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-34]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Mobility is held static, with no behavioural reduction, no border closure and no seasonality in transmissibility."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-35]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "No vaccination is modelled."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-36]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Samples with distance ≤ 5 % are accepted, and trajectories must have at least 3,674 true cumulative cases by 31 July."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-37]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Reporting of new cases is assumed proportional to isolation with a constant factor per trajectory."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-38]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The early epidemic size is only loosely constrained, which is why the intervals are wide."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-39]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The model occupancy of the hospital and isolation compartments is not compared with the reported number of patients in isolation in this report. The comparison is deferred until the definition of the reported figure and the length of stay in treatment centres have been checked."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-17]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "They are fitted to reported counts up to 30 September 2026 and validated on a single held-out count (10 October)."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-40]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Fewer simulations are accepted under 75 % isolation, so its estimates are less precise."
[^epistorm-2026b-projections-11]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Projections"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The data capture the slowdown itself, not yet its outcome."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-14]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "- **Data** DRC cumulative reported cases, 31 July – 30 September 2026 (fitted); 10 October (held out)."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-9]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The distance is the mean absolute percentage error over the increments since 31 July at 11, 20 and 25 August and 1, 9, 21 and 30 September 2026 (+882, +1,701, +2,039, +2,576, +3,268, +4,099, +4,626 cases)."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-10]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The projected reported count is Reported(t) = 3,674 + c × [I_iso(t) − I_iso(31 Jul)], where I_iso(t) is the simulated cumulative number of isolated symptomatic onsets that have been reported by day t, given the reporting delay (below)."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-11]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The 10 October count (8,978) is held out for validation."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-9]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "As of 10 October 2026, DRC has reported 8,978 cumulative cases and 4,336 cumulative deaths (case-fatality ratio ≈ 48 %) across 65 health zones in seven provinces, with 945 patients in isolation [1,2]."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-12]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Three calibration chains share these priors and differ in the evidence imposed: one conditioned on 2–4 early cross-border onsets in Uganda (23,638 trajectories), one conditioned on 2–80 early cumulative onsets in Beni (32,541) and one conditioned on both (7,235)."
[^epistorm-2026b-executive-summary-15]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Executive Summary"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Uganda has reported no new cases, and its total remains 20 laboratory-confirmed cases including 2 deaths [3]. One case has been reported in Kenya, with no local transmission."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-13]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Compartmental dwell times and severity values are based on literature."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-14]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "[9] are the source for the original 2007–2008 Uganda BVD outbreak data on which some of the BVD-specific natural-history estimates rest."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-15]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "Isolation-pathway case-finding timings (*T_iso* and *T_he*) follow the 2018 North Kivu RVEAP response [6]."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-16]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The compartmental structure follows a Legrand-like SEIHFR scheme [7,8]."
[^epistorm-2026b-methods-17]: [Epistorm (2026b) "Methods"](https://epistorm.github.io/EBV2026/report9_transmission.html): "The delay from symptom onset to report is assumed to follow a gamma distribution with a mean of 5 days and a standard deviation of 3.5 days, a typical delay from symptom onset to admission to a hospital or ETC and consistent with the onset-to-HCF time of the routine care pathway in the model (5 days)."
[^klein-2026-abstract-1]: [Klein et al. (2026) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "We fit a Bayesian spatial branching process to public situation reports through 2 August 2026, with Flowminder movement and a latent-burden anchor."
[^klein-2026-abstract-2]: [Klein et al. (2026) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Because the observed history did not identify future control, we assumed a national response ramp yielding West-Africa-scale status-quo burden and replayed 2,000 posterior trees with 5-100 zone-level PCR laboratories, deployment delays of 0-180 days, and postmortem rapid diagnostic test (RDT) coverage."
[^klein-2026-abstract-3]: [Klein et al. (2026) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Speed dominated: 35 PCR laboratories averted a median 49% of future deaths if deployed immediately, 38% after 14 days, and 11% after 60 days, with 90% prediction intervals of 30-65%, 23-53%, and 5-20%, respectively."
[^klein-2026-abstract-4]: [Klein et al. (2026) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Placement also mattered: with a 30-day delay, model-informed placement averted 18-33% of future deaths with 20-100 laboratories, versus 1-8% under random placement."
[^klein-2026-3-results-2]: [Klein et al. (2026) "3. Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Under the status quo plus the selected national response ramp, the simulated outbreak reached a median of 31,615 cumulative infections, 14,256 deaths, and 110 invaded health zones by 540 days after 2 August 2026."
[^klein-2026-3-results-3]: [Klein et al. (2026) "3. Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Of those deaths, a median 4,816 had already been locked in by the calibration horizon, leaving a median 9,360 future deaths that could still be averted."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-1]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The calibration horizon was 2 August 2026, at which point the model matched against 3,802 cumulative confirmed cases and 1,707 cumulative confirmed deaths."
[^klein-2026-abstract-5]: [Klein et al. (2026) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Postmortem RDTs partly filled gaps left by live-case detection, increasing future deaths averted from 26% to 41% with 20 laboratories, a 14-day delay, and 75% coverage."
[^klein-2026-3-results-4]: [Klein et al. (2026) "3. Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "In a matched 2,000-draw replay of the canonical continuation from each posterior state, the status quo seeded a median 32 new health zones after 2 August 2026. Model-placed PCR laboratories reduced that to 29 new zones with 20 laboratories, 26 with 50 laboratories, and 25 with 100 laboratories."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-7]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The epidemic was simulated as an individual-level stochastic branching process. Each infection has a parent, an infection date, a health zone, an incubation period, an illness duration, a fatal or nonfatal outcome, PCR and clinical detection states, an isolation state, a safeburial state, and a list of its secondary infections."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-8]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "For each offspring infection, the destination health zone was local with probability '1 -jump_prob;' otherwise the destination was drawn from the parent's row-normalised Flowminder outflow row."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-9]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The calibration also used an external latent-burden anchor from the EpiForecasts BVD-OutbreakSize analysis, which estimates infections, onsets, deaths, reporting fractions, and a time-varying reproduction number from the INSP situation reports and Uganda export data."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-10]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The status quo was calibrated with a pseudo-marginal MCMC sampler (Andrieu and Roberts, 2009) implemented with 'emcee' (Foreman-Mackey et ah, 2013)."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-11]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "We introduced a possible national reduction in transmission after the 2 August 2026 calibration horizon to represent broad behavioral change and non-diagnostic response activities that would be invisible to the likelihood."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-12]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2.
