[^klein-2026-2-methods-13]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Each diagnostic scenario was evaluated by replaying the same posterior tree used for the matched status-quo simulation. Both simulations shared the same per-case random numbers; the intervention pruned only transmissions that would have been prevented by an earlier PCR result, isolation event, traced contact, or safe burial."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-14]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "*\Nc* evaluated newly capacitated PCR laboratories placed in health zones not served by status-quo laboratories. Budgets were 5, 10, 20, 35, 50, 75, or 100 laboratories."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-15]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "A lazy CELF optimizer (Leskovec et ah, 2007) selected laboratories by marginal deaths averted under common random numbers; random placement was used as the comparator."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-3]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Deployment delays of 0, 14, 30, 60, 90, 120, and 180 days after the calibration horizon measured the penalty for late roll-out."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-16]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Postmortem RDT scenarios raised baseline postmortem testing to 50% or 75% coverage of otherwise undiagnosed deaths, either inside the PCR-machine footprint or everywhere."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-17]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "In a sample-transport sensitivity analysis, unserved health zones could ship specimens to the nearest new laboratory, receiving an intermediate PCR access probability and a road-time-based turnaround delay rather than full local-lab access."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-21]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The parameters governing this ramp-down were chosen against a 540-day forward anchor based on the 2014-2016 West Africa Ebola Zaire confirmed-case burden."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-22]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The primary scenario reached half of its effect after 90 days and used a final scale of 0.25 with Hill exponent 2."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-3]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "It is chosen, not fitted, because the likelihood is exactly blind to its parameters inside the fitted window."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-4]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "If nondiagnostic control were much faster than assumed here, the epidemic would be smaller and fewer deaths would remain for decentralized diagnostics to avert. If transmission were larger or more sustained, the window for PCR placement would widen and spatial diagnostics could have more absolute impact."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-5]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "We expect the relative ranking of strategies to be less sensitive than the absolute magnitude of benefit: speed, placement, and postmortem gap-filling all follow from diagnosis breaking future transmission chains, but the delay curves and optimal laboratory budgets would change across smaller and larger futures."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-19]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Offspring infection times were assigned from a gamma generation-interval distribution with mean 15.3 days and standard deviation 9.3 days, matching the West African Ebola Zaire serial-interval estimate used as a generation-time proxy by WHO Ebola Response Team and the BVD-OutbreakSize model (WHO Ebola Response Team, 2014; Abbott et al., 2026)."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-20]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Incubation periods were lognormal with mean 6.3 days and standard deviation 3.5 days, using the BDBV incubation mean from the 2007-2008 Uganda outbreak (MacNeil et al., 2010)."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-23]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Fatal infections had onset-to-death times drawn from a gamma distribution with mean 12 days; nonfatal infections had onset-to-recovery times drawn from a gamma distribution with mean 16 days."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-24]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "These illness-duration parameters, as well as the assumption that 37% of a fatal case's transmission potential can fall in a 0.8-day postmortem period, were fixed naturalhistory assumptions informed by Ebola case-series data and field interpretation and varied only in scenario sensitivity checks; fixed values, priors, and posterior summaries are listed in Appendix."
[^klein-2026-appendix-1-key-parameters-1]: [Klein et al. (2026) "Appendix 1: Key Parameters"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "| $k$ | Negative-binomial offspring dispersion | 0.372 | Fixed | Fixed after weak identification in earlier fits |."
[^klein-2026-3-results-5]: [Klein et al. (2026) "3. Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Its median 'RO' was 2.63. median fitted infection fatality ratio was 0.46, and the realised latest 'Rt' in a 200-draw diagnostic was 1.11."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-25]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "For each offspring infection, the destination health zone was local with probability '1 -jump_prob;' otherwise the destination was drawn from the parent's row-normalised Flowminder outflow row. The fitted 'jump_prob' was shared across health zones; Flowminder flows determined the relative destination probabilities conditional on a between-zone jump."
[^klein-2026-3-results-6]: [Klein et al. (2026) "3. Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The median bctwccn-zonc jump probability was 0.19, implying substantial movement among small health-zone units."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-6]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "It also creates the main per-zone interpretability caveat: the model places cases where infection occurred and does not include explicit referral to regional hospitals, nosocomial amplification, or facility-level transmission."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-26]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Susceptible depletion was ignored, which is a reasonable approximation at the fitted attack rates and health-zone populations, but a limitation for very prolonged or very large future trajectories."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-27]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The primary analysis assumed that routine sample transport between health zones was highly challenging: a PCR laboratory primarily served the health zone in which it was placed."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-28]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Health zones with status-quo laboratories were therefore treated as already served and were excluded from new laboratory placement, leaving 462 candidate zones."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-29]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "PCR sensitivity was not modeled as a separate failure process: a sampled true BDBV infection was assumed to be PCR-confirmed."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-30]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "This representation treats tracing as an offspring-level probability rather than as an explicit contact graph."
[^klein-2026-3-results-7]: [Klein et al. (2026) "3. Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Contact tracing from a PCR-confirmcd index was inferred to intercept a median 69% of its offspring."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-31]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "RDT scenarios then increased postmortem testing to at least 50% or 75% coverage of otherwise undiagnosed deaths and assumed 80% sensitivity."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-32]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Decedents with a positive RDT were assumed to receive a safe and dignified burial without additional modeled delay, so the channel remained imperfect but a positive test was actionable."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-7]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The scenarios also assume demand for decentralized care."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-8]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Limited care seeking would weaken this mechanism, although demand may rise if better local service makes early presentation more useful and trusted."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-9]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The model is intentionally stylized. It ignores susceptible depletion, omits onward transmission after importations to neighboring countries, represents contact tracing as a probability on true offspring rather than an explicit observed contact network, has no healthcare-facility compartment or nosocomial transmission channel, and routes new infections through Flowminder movement rather than through patient referral."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-10]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Those choices keep the simulation fast enough for posterior counterfactuals but soften facility-level siting conclusions. The model should be interpreted as ranking broad frontier risk and mechanism sizes, not as a deterministic list of which exact clinics must receive the next devices."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-11]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "It is chosen, not fitted, because the likelihood is exactly blind to its parameters inside the fitted window. All diagnostic effects reported here are therefore marginal to that assumed national ramp."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-12]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The size of the baseline future also affects the numerical scale of every counterfactual."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-13]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The exact timing of that decline is tied to the assumed future response trajectory, but the mechanism is general."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-14]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The intervention analysis was also limited to one primary sample-transport assumption."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-15]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "If a zone assigned to an existing laboratory is not served quickly in practice, the model will incorrectly assign it a high PCR probability and then conclude that adding a laboratory there has little value."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-16]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The placement result should also be interpreted as a fixed allocation problem. The optimizer was given the posterior at one decision time and asked where a fixed number of laboratories should go."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-17]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Field placement is more reactive: response teams learn where new clusters are occurring, which roads are passable, and which laboratories have become backlogged before the next tranche of capacity arrives."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-2]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The placement objective was also national: it minimized expected burden inside DRC."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-18]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "We do not represent faster notification from postmortem positives back to the local awareness process because the counterfactual replay engine does not propagate posthoc state changes across generations except through offspring pruning. This makes RDT effects one-sided lower bounds for any pathway that would also accelerate community recognition or contact tracing."
[^klein-2026-4-discussion-19]: [Klein et al. (2026) "4. Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Finally, the model considered epidemiologic benefit but not cost. It can estimate marginal infections, deaths, and health-zone invasions averted by decentralized PCR or postmortem RDT, but it cannot determine whether a particular mix of laboratories, staff, sample transport, and burial teams is cost-effective."
[^klein-2026-1-introduction-1]: [Klein et al. (2026) "1. Introduction"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "By 2 August 2026, the public INSP/INRB-UMIE record used by this project contained 3,802 confirmed cases and 1,707 confirmed deaths across 51 health zones, making the 2026 epidemic unprecedented for BDBV in both absolute burden and geographic scale."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-2]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "The model was calibrated to the full public INSP/INRB-UMIE 2026 DRC BDBV time series, pinned locally at BDBV2026-Data commit '60f6701 (build-2026-08-05-5e9266d' , SitRep 81; data to 3 August 2026)."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-18]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Laboratory activation dates, approximate PCR turnaround tiers, early Bunia and Rwampara capacity, and Ituri PCR throughput anchors reported after declaration of the Public Health Emergency of International Concern were compiled from the public situation-report laboratory sections."
[^klein-2026-2-methods-5]: [Klein et al. (2026) "2. Methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.09.29.26364338.full-text): "Province-level event rates from ACLED (Armed Conflict Location & Event Data), accessed 5 August 2026, were used to derive the fitted operating-disruption score: events over the 12 months ending 25 July 2026 were divided by provincial population, transformed into a normalized 0-1 score, and assigned to all health zones in the province (Raleigh et ah, 2023)."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-abstract-1]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "The primary estimand was scenario-based expected introduction pressure, expressed on an expected-count scale, for infected school-age children reaching school in each health zone during a one-week window."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-abstract-2]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "The framework combined recent reported transmission, estimated school-age exposure and attendance, a surveillance/interception probability, and directed mobility-based importation."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-abstract-3]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "In the base scenario, the six-province combined scenario-based expected introduction pressure was 10.97; the probabilistic sensitivity median was 11.17, with a 2.5–97.5% sensitivity range of 5.56–20.97."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-abstract-4]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Bunia, Rwampara and Nizi had the highest base introduction pressures, followed by Katwa and Nia Nia."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-2]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "At the 11 August cut-off, the preferred base specification produced a combined scenario-based expected count of 10.97 school-system introductions across the six-province analytical scope during the modelled week."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-3]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Across 10,000 sensitivity draws, the median combined expected introduction count was 11.17, with a 2.5th–97.5th percentile sensitivity range of 5.56–20.97."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-4]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "This number is an expectation under the model assumptions, not a forecast that exactly eleven infected pupils will attend school during September."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-1]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Ituri accounted for approximately 8.47 of the 10.97 combined expected introductions, or about three quarters of the total modelled pressure. North Kivu contributed about 1.39 and Haut-Uélé about 1.03, while Tshopo, South Kivu and Bas-Uélé contributed much smaller absolute expectations."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-5]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Bunia had the highest base introduction expectation (λ=2.08; P≥1=87.4%), followed by Rwampara (λ=1.84; 84.1%) and Nizi (λ=1.32; 73.4%)."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-6]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Katwa in North Kivu and Nia Nia in Ituri each had a modelled probability of at least one introduction of approximately 50%."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-7]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "The median imported component was 1.31, with a corresponding sensitivity range of 0.51–3.28."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-8]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Changing the baseline q from 0.10 to 0.50 changed the total expected introduction count from approximately 4.39 to 21.95."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-9]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Bunia, Rwampara, Nizi, Katwa and Nia Nia remained in the top ten in essentially all simulations; Wamba, Bambu and Mongbwalu were also highly stable."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-10]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Recent seven-day local epidemic pressure achieved a pooled ROC-AUC of 0.848."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-11]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Using a weekly-equivalent 14-day pressure measure increased this discrimination to 0.885, indicating a modest benefit from smoothing recent fluctuations."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-12]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Adding mobility produced the largest improvement in geographic discrimination: pooled ROC-AUC rose to 0.952 with seven-day source pressure and 0.953 with fourteen-day pressure."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-13]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Thirteen of the 24 newly affected zones (54.2%) were already among the top 10 mobility-ranked unaffected zones and 18 (75.0%) were in the top 20."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-5]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "A simple one-week continuation assumption projected the next school-week case burden from the most recent reconstructed seven-day case count; a weekly-equivalent 14-day pressure measure was examined as a robustness sensitivity."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-6]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "For each health zone, projected cases were multiplied by the assumed 5–17-year case share and expected attendance to obtain the expected number of infected pupils who would otherwise attend. This quantity was then multiplied by qh, the probability that an infected child reaches school before recognition, isolation or exclusion from attendance."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-7]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "The mapping from λh,T to 1 − exp(−λh,T) is a conditional Poisson-process transformation used to express expected pressure as a model-implied scenario probability."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-8]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "For destination *h*, imported pressure was calculated as λimp,h = qh [fF Σo≠h Ao WFoh + fI Σo≠h Ao WIoh], where Ao is origin infected school-age attendance pressure before interception, wFoh and wIoh are Flowminder and IOM origin–destination shares, and fF and fI are scenario movement fractions."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-9]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "The destination health zone’s q adjustment was then applied."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-10]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "We conducted 10,000 Monte Carlo draws with a fixed random seed. Structural assumptions for the 5–17 case share, school attendance, baseline q, Flowminder movement fraction and IOM displacement fraction were drawn from split-triangular distributions with the base value as the median."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-11]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Week-to-week case-count variability was represented by a Poisson draw with mean equal to the most recent seven-day case count."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-12]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "For each origin, only information available on or before the cut-off was used to generate model scores, and subsequent reported health-zone activity was assessed over the following seven days."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-13]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Two outcomes were considered. First, future health-zone activity was defined as any reported increase in cumulative confirmed cases during the subsequent seven days. Second, geographic spread was defined as the first reported confirmed case in a health zone that had not yet reported a case at the cut-off."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-14]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Discrimination was summarized using ROC-AUC and average precision; retrieval of subsequently active or newly affected health zones was assessed using top-10 and top-20 capture; and Spearman rank correlation was used to compare model scores with the magnitude of subsequent reported case increments."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-15]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "As a secondary exploratory sensitivity, we compared three nested specifications: local epidemic pressure alone; local pressure with surveillance and access modifiers; and the same specification with directed mobility."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-16]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "We therefore analysed CFR separately using empirical-Bayes shrinkage toward a leave-one-out provincial comparator, with a prior effective sample size of 20. The resulting residuals were treated only as descriptive severity/response-capacity signals and did not modify λh,T or the primary health-zone ranking."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-17]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "To interpret the quantitative findings against the response environment immediately preceding the snapshot, we reviewed the ten most recent available national SitRep sources by report date (31 July–11 August 2026) [20]. Operational notes were treated as contemporaneous snapshots rather than a continuous qualitative series; non-mention of a problem was not interpreted as evidence of absence, and no contextual theme was converted into a numerical model weight."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-25]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "The base pre-isolation school-entry probability q was set to 0.25, with low and high scenarios of 0.10 and 0.50."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-26]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Health-zone q values were adjusted using analyst-specified structural modifiers for contact follow-up, testing access, health-site density, documented school-response activity and data quality: qh = clip(q0 × mcontact,h × mPCR,h × msite,h × mschool,h × mdata,h, 0.05, 0.75)."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-27]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "These weights are not fitted coefficients."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-28]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "In the absence of a complete harmonised health-zone school census, the population aged 5–17 years was estimated using a national age-structure share of 31.96%, derived from UNICEF/UN population age bands [21]."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-29]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Expected school attendance was represented with a base value of 56.6%, obtained by age-weighting UNICEF education indicators used in the analytical workbook (43% at age 5, 78% for primary-age children, 32% for lower-secondary age and 34% for upper-secondary age); low and high sensitivity values were 45% and 70% [21]."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-20]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "The proportion of confirmed cases occurring among persons aged 5–17 years was based on the latest usable standardised SitRep demographic snapshot (455 of 3,454 cases; 13.17%) and was varied from 10% to 16% in sensitivity analyses [20]."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-30]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "These quantities are exposure assumptions rather than observed attendance on 1 September and were therefore propagated as uncertain inputs."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-31]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "It is not an empirically calibrated probability forecast, and the Poisson assumption is not tested for clustered household, mobility or school-linked events (Fig 1)."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-32]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "For each origin, available non-self OD entries were normalized across the destinations represented in the study matrix; origins with no usable row contributed no imported pressure."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-discussion-1]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Flowminder and IOM channels may partly overlap; treating them as additive may double-count some movement, although a Flowminder-only sensitivity preserved the top-10 ranking and reduced total λ only from 10.974 to 10.736."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-18]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "By comparison, plausible changes in the mobility scaling moved the total much less."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-33]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Crude case-fatality ratios were not used as transmission drivers."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-34]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Operational notes were treated as contemporaneous snapshots rather than a continuous qualitative series; non-mention of a problem was not interpreted as evidence of absence, and no contextual theme was converted into a numerical model weight."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-35]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Structural assumptions for the 5–17 case share, school attendance, baseline q, Flowminder movement fraction and IOM displacement fraction were drawn from split-triangular distributions with the base value as the median."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-36]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "This Poisson step is a simple process-variation device and does not establish that introductions are independently Poisson in reality."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-abstract-6]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Abstract"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "The framework supports targeted geographic prioritisation and minimum school-health readiness, but its probabilities are model-implied scenario probabilities rather than calibrated forecasts or evidence for a single national open/close decision."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-37]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "The resulting 2.5th–97.5th percentile ranges are sensitivity intervals: they combine uncertainty under specified assumptions and simple process variation, and should not be interpreted as calibrated confidence or predictive intervals."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-2]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Because the formal rentrée was scheduled for 1 September 2026, and because administrative mobilisation and actual return-to-class timing may vary locally, the analysis should be interpreted as a pre-rentrée operational snapshot rather than a direct observation of September school attendance."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-discussion-2]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "School and pupil denominators are extrapolated rather than drawn from a complete harmonised health-zone school census. [^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-discussion-3]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Contact-follow-up and alert-investigation indicators are affected by reporting completeness."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-discussion-4]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Mobility data describe population movement rather than observed pupil or teacher travel, and missing or redacted flows can generate false reassurance."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-19]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Vuhovi received a zero mobility score because it was not represented as a destination in the available Flowminder matrix and had no compensating IOM flow. [^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-discussion-5]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "It may partly capture population size, urbanisation, proximity to affected areas, commercial connectivity, reporting intensity or health-system visibility, not only true infectious travel. [^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-16]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Of 151 analytical health zones, 96 were flagged as having no health-zone case series and 55 as having an OK/review source status."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-discussion-6]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "The retrospective validation assesses reported health-zone activity, not the unobserved event of an infected child actually entering a school."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-discussion-7]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "It cannot fully distinguish biological spread from geographic differences in testing access, reporting completeness, insecurity, response attention or data connectivity."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-20]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "These intervals reflect variation across a small number of origins and are not an external-validation confidence interval."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-21]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "This reinforces the within-sample ablation result, but it does not create an independent temporal or spatial holdout."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-38]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "The package does not fully reconstruct the original rolling-origin score matrices from dated health-zone case-count histories; this boundary is stated in S2 rather than implied away."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-discussion-8]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "The package provides hashes and source lineage for major files but does not constitute an independently double-extracted SitRep audit trail."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-discussion-9]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "The study also does not model within-school secondary transmission."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-discussion-10]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Available evidence is sufficient to identify these mechanisms, but not to calibrate a defensible school-level transmission model across 151 health zones."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-discussion-11]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Discussion"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "These contrasts are not modelled as a separate urban/rural coefficient because comparable school-level data are not available; they are part of the interpretation of what a health-zone introduction might mean."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-18]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Epidemiological data were drawn from DRC national SitReps and the INRB-UMIE BDBV2026-Data repository, which manually transcribes SitRep indicators and integrates contextual public-health response data [19,20]."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-19]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Recent seven-day reported case incidence was used as the principal epidemic-pressure signal."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-materials-and-methods-21]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Materials and methods"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Health-zone case histories were reconstructed from the reconciled direct-PDF series rather than from a single repository cumulative-case file."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-14]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "Across eight non-overlapping weekly validation origins, the primary analysis included 1,208 health-zone–origin observations, of which 200 showed reported case activity during the following seven days."

Expected school attendance was represented with a base value of 56.6%, obtained by age-weighting UNICEF education indicators used in the analytical workbook (43% at age 5, 78% for primary-age children, 32% for lower-secondary age and 34% for upper-secondary age); low and high sensitivity values were 45% and 70% [21]."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-results-17]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Results"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "The Flowminder study matrix had nonzero outgoing rows for 132 health zones and zero outgoing rows for 19; 138 destinations had nonzero incoming Flowminder shares and 13 had none. [^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-introduction-2]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Introduction"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "In May 2026, the Ministry of Education instructed schools and examination centres to reinforce handwashing, immediately report suspected cases and reorganise spaces to reduce close contact; the President subsequently directed intensified sensitisation in schools and universities [15,16]."
[^verheyden-mudogo-2026b-introduction-3]: [Verheyden and Mudogo (2026b) "Introduction"](https://www.medrxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.20.26360976.full-text): "As an older national structural benchmark, MICS 2018 indicators cited by UNICEF and Education Cannot Wait reported that only 38% of primary schools had access to water and that primary-school completion was 48% in rural areas compared with 79% in urban areas [11]."
